If
{\mathbb{M}}_{1}\leq_{\kappa}{\mathbb{M}}_{2}
and
A\subseteq{\mathbb{M}}_{2}

has cardinality

<\kappa
then for some
{\mathbb{N}}\in c\ell^{\kappa}_{is}({\mathcal{K}})
, we
have
A\subseteq{\mathbb{M}}_{1}+{\mathbb{N}}={\mathbb{M}}_{1}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}\leq_{\kappa}{\mathbb{M}}_{2}
.

(7)

The pair

({\rm Mod}_{{\mathfrak{m}},\kappa},\leq_{\kappa})

has the amalgamation property.

(8)

There is a cardinal
\chi
such that if
{\mathbb{M}}_{1}\subseteq{\mathbb{N}}
are in
{\rm{\rm Mod}}_{{\mathfrak{m}},\kappa}
, then there is
{\mathbb{M}}_{2}\in{\rm{\rm Mod}}_{{\mathfrak{m}},\kappa}
such that
{\mathbb{M}}_{1}\subseteq{\mathbb{M}}_{2}\leq_{\kappa}{\mathbb{N}}
and
|{\mathbb{M}}_{2}|\leq|{\mathbb{M}}_{1}|+\chi
.

Proof.

The lemma follows easily from Lemmas
3.18
and
3.19
.
∎

Definition 3.21
.

We say the bimodule

{\mathbb{N}}
is free as an

{\bf S}
–module
if

(a)

As an
{\bf R}
-module, it can be written as

\mathop{{\bigoplus}}\limits_{i<\alpha}{\mathbb{N}}_{i}
where each
{\mathbb{N}}_{i}
is a sub-
{\bf R}
-module
of
{\mathbb{N}}
.

(b)

If
{\mathbb{M}}
is a bimodule,
i<\alpha
and

g:{\mathbb{N}}_{i}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{M}}
is an
{\bf R}
-homomorphism,
then there is a unique
bimodule homomorphism
h:{\mathbb{N}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{M}}

extending
g
.

The next lemma can be proved as in Lemma
3.8
.

Lemma 3.22
.

Let
{\mathfrak{e}}\in{{\mathfrak{E}}}
.

(1)

There exists a formula

\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}\in\mathcal{L}_{\infty,\infty}^{cpe}(\tau_{{\bf R}})
such that
for an
{\bf R}
-module

{\mathbb{M}}
,

{\mathbb{M}}\models\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}(x)
iff
for some
{\mathbb{M}}^{\prime}
with

{\mathbb{M}}\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}^{\prime}
,

{\mathbb{M}}^{\prime}\models\varphi^{{\mathfrak{e}},\infty}_{n}(x)
;
equivalently there is an
{\bf R}
-module
homomorphism from
{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}

into
{\mathbb{M}}^{\prime}
mapping

x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
to
x
.

(2)

There exists
\psi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}\in\mathcal{L}_{\infty,\infty}(\tau_{{\bf R}})
such that
for a bimodule

{\mathbb{M}}
,

{\mathbb{M}}\models\psi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}(x)
iff
for some
{\mathbb{M}}^{\prime}
with

{\mathbb{M}}\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}^{\prime}
,

{\mathbb{M}}^{\prime}\models\psi^{{\mathfrak{e}},\infty}_{n}(x)
.

The above lemma suggests the following:

Definition 3.23
.

By
\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}(x)
we mean
\bigwedge\limits_{n<\omega}\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}(x)
and
\overline{\varphi}^{\mathfrak{e}}:=\langle\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}:n<\omega\rangle
. Similarly, we define

\psi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}(x):=\bigwedge\limits_{n<\omega}\psi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}(x)
and let
\overline{\psi}^{\mathfrak{e}}:=\langle\psi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}:n<\omega\rangle
.

Definition 3.24
.

(1)

A bimodule
{\mathbb{M}}
is called
{{\mathfrak{E}}}
-closed if for
every
{\mathfrak{e}}\in{{\mathfrak{E}}}
,
n<\omega
and every

x\in{\mathbb{M}}
we have

{\mathbb{M}}\models\psi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}(x)\iff{\mathbb{M}}\models\psi^{{\mathfrak{e}},\infty}_{n}(x).

(2)

We say that
\mathfrak{e}
is non-trivial for
{{\mathfrak{m}}}

if for every
n<\omega
there exists
{\mathbb{M}}\in{\mathcal{K}}^{\mathfrak{m}}
such that

{\mathbb{M}}_{*}^{\mathfrak{m}}\oplus{\mathbb{M}}\models(\exists x)[\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}(x)\wedge\neg\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}(x)]
.

Fact 3.25
.

It is easily seen that if
{\mathfrak{e}}\in{\mathfrak{E}}^{{\mathfrak{m}}}

is adequate and each

{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
is
finitely generated (as
an
{\bf R}
-module), then
{\mathfrak{e}}
is
non-trivial for
{{\mathfrak{m}}}
.

Convention 3.26
.

From now on
we fix a
context
{\mathfrak{m}}=({\mathcal{K}},{\mathbb{M}}_{*},{\mathfrak{E}},{\bf R},{\bf S},{\bf T})

which
is non-trivial which means:

(a)

{\mathcal{K}}\neq\emptyset
,

(b)

{\mathfrak{E}}\neq\emptyset
,

(c)

every
{\mathfrak{e}}\in{\mathfrak{E}}_{\infty,\kappa}
is non-trivial for
{\mathfrak{m}}
.

Definition 3.27
.

Let

{\mathfrak{e}}\in{{\mathfrak{E}}}
and
\bar{\varphi}
is adequate with respect to

\mathfrak{e}
.

(1)

Suppose

{\mathbb{M}}
is a bimodule and
{\bf h_{1}},{\bf h_{2}}:{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}\to{\mathbb{M}}
are bimodule homomorphisms. We define

{\mathbb{L}}^{{\mathfrak{e}},\bar{\varphi},{\bf h_{1}},{\bf h_{2}}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}}):=\{z\in\varphi_{n}({\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}):{\bf h_{1}}(z)={\bf h_{2}}(z)\ \mod\ \varphi_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}})\}.

For simplicity, and if there is no danger of confusion, we set

{\mathbb{L}}^{{\mathfrak{e}},\bar{\varphi},{\bf h_{1}},{\bf h_{2}}}_{n}:={\mathbb{L}}^{{\mathfrak{e}},\bar{\varphi},{\bf h_{1}},{\bf h_{2}}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}}).

If
\bar{\varphi}=\bar{\varphi}^{{\mathfrak{e}},\kappa}
we may write

{\mathbb{L}}^{{\mathfrak{e}},\kappa,{\bf h_{1}},{\bf h_{2}}}_{n}
and if
\kappa=\aleph_{0}
we may omit it.

(2)

Let

{\mathbb{L}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}[{\mathfrak{m}}]:=\bigcap\big\{{\mathbb{L}}^{{\mathfrak{e}},{\bf h_{1}},{\bf h_{2}}}_{n}:\text{~for
some~}{\mathbb{M}}\in{\mathcal{K}}\cup\{{\mathbb{M}}_{\ast}\},{\bf h_{1}},{\bf h_{2}}:{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}\to{\mathbb{M}}\text{~are bimodule }

\text{homomorphisms and~}{\bf h_{1}}(x_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}})={\bf h_{2}}(x_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}})\big\}.

We may write
{\mathbb{L}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}:={\mathbb{L}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}[{\mathfrak{m}}]
, when
{\mathfrak{m}}

is clear from the context.

(3)

{\mathbb{L}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}[\mathcal{K}]
, is defined similarly but
we only require
{\mathbb{M}}\in{\mathcal{K}}
.

Lemma 3.28
.

Let
{\mathfrak{e}}\in{{\mathfrak{E}}}
. Then, there are bimodules
{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}}
,

{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
and
{\mathbb{K}}_{n}
, embeddings
{\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}:{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}}
, an element
x=x_{\mathfrak{e}}
, an
embedding
{\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}:{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{0}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}}

and

x_{n}:=x_{{\mathfrak{e}},n}\in{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}}
such that:

(a)

\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n})\cap\sum_{m\neq n}\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{m})=\{0\}
,

(b)

For each
{\mathbb{K}}_{n}
we have:

{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}}=\left(\sum_{\ell<n}\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell})\right)+{\mathbb{K}}_{n}={\bigoplus}_{\ell<n}\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell})\oplus{\mathbb{K}}_{n},

and
{\mathbb{K}}_{n}
is a direct sum of copies of
{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{m}
’s.

(c)

\sum_{n<\omega}\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n})
is not a direct
summand of
{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}}
; moreover, for some
embeddings
{\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}:{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}}

satisfying:

(\ast)_{1}

x_{n}=\sum_{\ell<n}{\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell}\big(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell}\big)\in\sum_{\ell<n}\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell})
,

(\ast)_{2}

x-x_{n}\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}})
,

(\ast)_{3}

{\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}={\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{0}
,

(\ast)_{4}

{\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}\big(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{0}\big)=x
,

(\ast)_{5}

x\notin\sum_{n<\omega}\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n})+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}})
.

(d)

{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}}
is the direct sum of the copies

\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n})
of
{\mathbb{N}}_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}
.

Proof.

Set
{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}}:=\mathop{{\bigoplus}}\limits_{n<\omega}{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
and denote the natural embedding from
{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
into
{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}}

by
{\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
.
In particular,

{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}}=\mathop{{\bigoplus}}\limits_{n<\omega}\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}),

i.e., (d) holds. We define
{\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}:{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{M}}
,
for all
n<\omega
, as follows, where
g^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n,n+1}
is defined as in
Definition
3.1
(3):

{\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}(y)={\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}(y)-{\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n+1}(g^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n,n+1}(y))\mbox{ for every }y\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}.

As
{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}}=\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n})\oplus\mathop{{\bigoplus}}\limits_{\ell\neq n}\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell})
,
we observe that
{\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
is a bimodule embedding. Set also

{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}:=\sum\limits_{\ell<n}\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell}).

The next claim can be proved easily.

Claim 3.29
.

Adopt the above notation. Then,
for each
n
,

\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n})\oplus\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n+1})=\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n})\oplus\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n+1}).

In particular,
{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}}=\mathop{{\bigoplus}}\limits_{\ell<n}\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n})\oplus\mathop{{\bigoplus}}\limits_{\ell\geq n}\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell})
for all
n
.

We set
{\mathbb{K}}_{n}:=\mathop{{\bigoplus}}\limits_{\ell\geq n}\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell})
. In view of Claim
3.29
, the items
(a)
,
(b)
and
(d)
of Lemma
3.28
are hold.
Next we shall show that
x:={\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{0}((x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{0}))
and

x_{n}:=\sum\limits_{\ell<n}{\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell})
are as required in Lemma
3.28
(c).
This implies the first
statement of
(*)_{1}
in item
(c).
Trivially
(*)_{3}
and
(\ast)_{4}
are true.
Now,

\begin{array}[]{ll}x&={\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{0}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{0})\\
&={\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{0}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{0})+{\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{1}(g^{\mathfrak{e}}_{0,1}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{0}))\\
&={\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{0}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{0})+{\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{1}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{1})\\
&={\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{0}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{0})+{\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{1}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{1})+{\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{2}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{2}).\end{array}

By induction on

n
we have

x=\sum\limits_{\ell<n}{\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell})+{\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n})=x_{n}+{\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}).

Clearly,
x_{n}\in\mathop{{\bigoplus}}\limits_{\ell<n+1}\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell})\subseteq\mathop{{\bigoplus}}\limits_{\ell<\omega}\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell})
and by the choice of
{\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}

the second term is in
\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}})
,
so
(*)_{1}+(*)_{2}
of clause (c)
holds.
We shall now show that

x\notin\sum\limits_{\ell<\omega}\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell})+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}})
,
i.e.,
(*)_{5}
holds.

Suppose by contradiction that there is an

n\geq 1

such that
x\in y+\sum\limits_{\ell<n}\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell})
,
where
y\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}})
.

We now define a bimodule endomorphism
{\bf f}_{n}\in{\rm End}({\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}})
. As
{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}}=\mathop{{\bigoplus}}\limits_{\ell<\omega}\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell})
, it
is clearly
enough to define each
{\bf f}_{n}\restriction\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(f^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell})
, for
n\geq 1,
separately.
Recall that
{\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell}
is
one-to-one. Let
z\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell}
. We define

{\bf f}_{n}({\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell}(z))=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}(g^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell,n}(z))&\mbox{if }\ell\leq n,\\
0&\mbox{otherwise }\end{array}\right.

Now, we bring the following claim:

Claim 3.30
.

1)

If
\ell\not=n
, then
{\bf f}_{n}\restriction\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell})

is identically zero.

2)

If
\ell=n
, then
{\bf f}_{n}(x)={\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}).

Proof.

1): For
\ell>n
this is trivial, so suppose that
\ell<n
. Let

z\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell}
. Then
{\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell}(z)={\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell}(z)-{\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{{\ell}+1}(g^{\mathfrak{e}}_{{\ell},{\ell}+1}(z))
. So,

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\bf f}_{n}({\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell}(z))&={\bf f}_{n}\left({\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell}(z)-{\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{{\ell}+1}(g^{\mathfrak{e}}_{{\ell},{\ell}+1}(z))\right)\\
&={\bf f}_{n}({\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell}(z))-{\bf f}_{n}({\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{{\ell}+1}(g^{\mathfrak{e}}_{{\ell},{\ell}+1}(z)))\\
&={\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}(g^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell,n}(z))-{\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}(g^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell,\ell+1}(g^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell+1,n}(z)))\\
&={\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}(g^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell,n}(z))-{\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}(g^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell,n}(z))\\
&=0.\end{array}

The third equation holds because of clause (e) of Lemma
3.28
.

2): It is enough to recall
{\bf f}_{n}(x)={\bf f}_{n}({\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{0}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{0}))={\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}(g^{\mathfrak{e}}_{0,n}({\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{0}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{0}))={\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}).

∎

Since
x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}\notin\varphi_{\omega}({\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n})
, we have
{\bf f}_{n}(x)={\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n})\neq 0
. Indeed, we have

x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}\notin\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}(\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}))=\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}})\cap\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}).

But
x-y\in\sum\limits_{\ell<n}\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell})
and

{\bf f}_{n}{\restriction}\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n})
is zero, so
{\bf f}_{n}(x-y)=0
. Hence

{\bf f}_{n}(x)={\bf f}_{n}(y)
. However
{\bf f}_{n}(x)\notin\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}})
. Thus,

{\bf f}_{n}(y)\notin\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}})
.
Recall that
y\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}})
. Therefore,
{\bf f}_{n}(y)\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}})
. This contradiction completes the proof of Lemma
3.28
.
∎

Lemma 3.31
.

(1)

Let
x\in{\mathbb{M}}_{1}
,

{\mathfrak{e}}\in{\mathfrak{E}}
and

\varphi\in\{\tilde{\varphi}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\alpha},\tilde{\psi}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\alpha}:\alpha\leq\omega\}
. If
{\mathbb{M}}_{1}\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{2}
, then

{\mathbb{M}}_{1}\models\varphi(x)\iff{\mathbb{M}}_{2}\models\varphi(x).

(2)

If
{\mathbb{M}}_{\ast}\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{1}
and
{{\mathfrak{E}}}^{\prime}\subseteq{{\mathfrak{E}}}
,
then there is an
{\mathbb{M}}_{2}
with the following properties:

(a)

{\mathbb{M}}_{1}\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{2}
,

(b)

\|{\mathbb{M}}_{2}\|\leq\|{\mathbb{M}}_{1}\|+\|{\mathfrak{m}}\|+|{{\mathfrak{E}}}^{\prime}|
,

(c)

if
x\in{\mathbb{M}}_{1}
,
n<\omega
and
{\mathfrak{e}}\in{{\mathfrak{E}}}^{\prime}
then

{\mathbb{M}}_{2}\models\varphi^{{\mathfrak{e}},\infty}_{n}(x)\iff{\mathbb{M}}_{1}\models\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}[x],

(d)

{\mathbb{M}}_{2}
is the free sum of
\{{\mathbb{M}}_{2,i}:i<i^{\ast}\}\cup\{{\mathbb{M}}_{1}\}
where each

{\mathbb{M}}_{2,i}
isomorphic to some member of
{\mathcal{K}}
,

(e)

For each
{\mathbb{N}}\in\mathcal{K},
there are

||{\mathbb{M}}_{2}||
-bimodules from
\{{\mathbb{M}}_{2,i}:i<i^{\ast}\}

each of them being
isomorphic to
{\mathbb{N}}
.

Proof.

Clause (1) can be proved easily. To prove (2), let
\kappa:=|{\mathbb{M}}_{1}\|+\|{\mathfrak{m}}\|+|{{\mathfrak{E}}}^{\prime}|
and set

{\mathbb{M}}_{2}:={\mathbb{M}}_{1}\oplus\bigoplus_{{\mathbb{N}}\in\mathcal{K}}\bigoplus_{i<\kappa}{\mathbb{M}}_{i}^{{\mathbb{N}}},

where each
{\mathbb{M}}_{i}^{{\mathbb{N}}}
is isomorphic to
{\mathbb{N}}.
It is easily seen that
{\mathbb{M}}_{2}

is as required.
∎

We now introduce the notion of (semi) nice construction. This concept plays an important role for the
proof of our main results.

Definition 3.32
.

Suppose
{\mathfrak{m}}=(\mathcal{K},{\mathbb{M}}_{*},{\mathfrak{E}},{\bf R},{\bf S},{\bf T})
is a context,

\lambda\geq\kappa=\mathop{\mathrm{cf}}(\kappa)>\kappa({\mathfrak{E}})
. Also,
\lambda=\lambda^{\kappa}
and for all
\alpha<\lambda
,

|\alpha|^{\aleph_{0}}<\lambda
. Here,

\bar{\gamma}^{*}=\langle\gamma^{*}_{\alpha}:\alpha\leq\kappa\rangle

is increasing continuous
and
S\subseteq S^{\kappa}_{\aleph_{0}}
7
7

7

where
S^{\kappa}_{\aleph_{0}}=\{\alpha<\kappa:\mathop{\mathrm{cf}}(\alpha)=\aleph_{0}\}
.
is stationary such that
S^{\kappa}_{\aleph_{0}}\setminus S
is stationary as well. Let also
\langle\mathfrak{e}_{\alpha}:\alpha<|{\mathfrak{E}}|\rangle
be a fixed enumeration of
{\mathfrak{E}}
and
\emph{rep}(\mathcal{K})=\langle{\mathbb{N}}^{\beta}:\beta<|\mathcal{K}/\cong|\rangle

be an enumeration of
\mathcal{K}
up to isomorphism.

A
weakly semi-nice construction

\mathcal{A}
for
(\lambda,{{\mathfrak{m}}},S,\bar{\gamma}^{*})

is a sequence
\bar{\mathbb{M}}=\langle{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}:\alpha\leq\kappa\rangle
together with the
other objects mentioned below satisfying the following
conditions

(A)

{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}
is a bimodule whose universe is the ordinal

\gamma_{\alpha}^{*}
for
\alpha\leq\kappa
.

(B)

\alpha<\beta\ \Rightarrow\ {\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}\subseteq{\mathbb{M}}_{\beta}
.

(C)

if
\delta\leq\kappa
is a limit ordinal, then

{\mathbb{M}}_{\delta}=\bigcup\limits_{\alpha<\delta}{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}
.

(D)

{\mathbb{M}}_{0}={\mathbb{M}}_{\ast}
.

(E)

for every
\alpha\in\kappa\setminus S
,
{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha+1}={\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}\oplus\mathop{\bigoplus}\limits_{t\in J_{\alpha}}{\mathbb{N}}^{\alpha}_{t}

where

(a)

|J_{\alpha}|\leq\lambda
,

(b)

J_{\alpha}\cap(\bigcup\limits_{\beta<\alpha}J_{\beta})=\emptyset,

(c)

\mathop{\mathrm{cf}}(\gamma^{*}_{\alpha+1})<\lambda\Leftrightarrow|J_{\alpha}|<\lambda
,

(d)

for every
t\in J_{\alpha}
there is
\beta<|\mathcal{K}/\cong|

such that
{\mathbb{N}}_{t}^{\alpha}\cong{\mathbb{N}}^{\beta}
, i.e., every
{\mathbb{N}}_{t}^{\alpha}
is isomorphic to some member of
\mathcal{K},

(e)

for every
\beta<|\mathcal{K}/\cong|
there are
||{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}||
-many
t\in J_{\alpha}
such that
{\mathbb{N}}_{t}^{\alpha}\cong{\mathbb{N}}^{\beta}
.

By the assumption (b), for each
t\in\bigcup\limits_{\beta\leq\alpha}J_{\beta}
there is a unique

\beta\leq\alpha
such that
t\in J_{\beta}
; so we may replace
{\mathbb{N}}^{\beta}_{t}
by
{\mathbb{N}}_{t}.
Also given

t\in J_{\alpha}
, there is a unique
{\mathbb{N}}_{t}^{\rm so}\in\emph{rep}(\mathcal{K})
such that
{\mathbb{N}}_{t}^{\rm so}\cong{\mathbb{N}}_{t}^{\alpha}
. Also, we use the notations

h_{t}
and
h_{\alpha,t}:{\mathbb{N}}_{t}^{\rm so}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathbb{N}}_{t}^{\alpha}
for the mentioned isomorphism.

(F)

for
\delta\in S
, either the demand in
clause
8
8

8

we can ignore this
first possibility as we can
just shrink
S
if the result is stationary.

(E) holds
or else there are
\gamma^{**}_{\delta}<\delta
and

\langle({\mathfrak{e}}_{s},{\mathbb{P}}_{s},\bar{\alpha}_{s},\bar{t}_{s},\bar{g}_{s},h_{s},q_{s}):s\in J_{\delta}\rangle=\langle{\mathfrak{e}}^{\delta}_{s},{\mathbb{P}}^{\delta}_{s},\bar{\alpha}^{\delta}_{s},\bar{t}^{\delta}_{s},\bar{g}^{\delta}_{s},h^{\delta}_{s},q^{\delta}_{s}):s\in J_{\delta}\rangle

such that
J_{\delta}\cap(\bigcup_{\alpha<\delta}J_{\alpha})=\emptyset
and

(a)

{\mathfrak{e}}_{s}\in{\mathfrak{E}}
and in fact

{\mathfrak{e}}_{s}\in\{{\mathfrak{e}}_{\beta}:\beta<\gamma^{**}_{\delta}

and
\beta<|{\mathfrak{E}}|\}
,

(b)

\bar{\alpha}_{s}=\langle\alpha_{s,n}:n<\omega\rangle
,

(c)

\bar{t}=\langle t_{s,n}:n<\omega\rangle
,

(d)

\langle\alpha_{s,n}:n<\omega\rangle
is an increasing sequence of ordinals bigger
than
\gamma^{**}_{\delta}
such that
\alpha_{s,n}\notin S
,

(e)

\delta=\mathop{\mathrm{sup}}\{\alpha_{s,n}:n<\omega\}
,

(f)

t_{s,n}\in J_{\alpha_{s,n}}
,

(g)

{\mathbb{N}}^{so}_{t_{s,n}}={\mathbb{N}}^{{\mathfrak{e}}_{s}}_{n}
,

(h)

if
s_{1}\not=s_{2}
are in
J_{\delta}
then the
sets

\{t_{s_{1},n}:n<\omega\},\{t_{s_{2},n}:n<\omega\}

are tree-like, i.e.,

•

\{t_{s_{1},n}:n<\omega\}\cap\{t_{s_{2},n}:n<\omega\}

is finite,

•

if
t_{s_{1},n_{1}}=t_{s_{2},n_{2}}
then
n_{1}=n_{2}
and

\bigwedge\limits_{n<n_{1}}t_{s_{1},n}=t_{s_{2},n}
,

(i)

\bar{g}_{s}=\langle g_{s,n}:n<\omega\rangle
,
where each
g_{s,n}
is a (bimodule) homomorphism from

{\mathbb{N}}^{{\mathfrak{e}}_{s}}_{n}

into
{\mathbb{M}}_{\gamma^{**}_{\delta}}
,

(j)

for
s\in J_{\delta}
,
h_{s}
is a bimodule homomorphism
from
{\mathbb{P}}^{{\mathfrak{e}}_{s}}
9
9

9

where
{\mathbb{P}}^{{\mathfrak{e}}_{s}}
is as in Lemma
3.28

Lemma 3.34
.

Let
\langle{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}:\alpha\leq\kappa\rangle
be a semi-nice
construction for

(\lambda,{{\mathfrak{m}}},S,\bar{\gamma}^{*})

and
\kappa({\mathfrak{E}}^{{{\mathfrak{m}}}})={\aleph_{0}}
,
(not used in part (1)).

(1)

if
\alpha\notin S
and
\alpha\leq\beta\leq\kappa
, then

{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\beta}
, i.e.,

{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}
is an almost direct
{\mathcal{K}}
-summand of

{\mathbb{M}}
with respect to
\aleph_{0}
.

(2)

If
\alpha\leq\beta\leq\kappa
and
n<\omega
then

\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha})={\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}\cap\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{\beta})
.

(3)

Assume

{\mathfrak{e}}\in{\mathfrak{E}}^{{{\mathfrak{m}}}}
,

\delta\in S
and clause (F) of Definition
3.32
holds for

\delta
. Suppose
s_{0},\ldots,s_{k(*)-1}
are distinct members of

J_{\delta}
. Then

(a)

for each
n
, the set

\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}(\langle{\mathbb{M}}_{\delta}\cup\bigcup\{{\mathbb{P}}_{s_{k}}^{\mathfrak{e}}:k<k(*)i\}\rangle_{{\mathbb{M}}_{\delta+1}})
is equal to

\langle{\mathbb{M}}_{\delta}\cup\bigcup\{{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{s_{k}}:k<k(*)\}\rangle_{{\mathbb{M}}_{\delta+1}})\cap\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa})\rangle.

(b)

Suppose
z_{\ell}\in{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}}
for
\ell<n,z\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\delta}
,
and
z+\sum\{h^{\delta}_{s_{k}}(z_{k}):k<k(*)\}\in\varphi_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{\delta+1})
. Then, there is
z^{\prime}_{k}\in\sum\{{\rm Rang}(h^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell}):\ell<\omega\}
such that the following two properties hold:

(b.1)

:

z_{k}-z^{\prime}_{k}\in\varphi_{n}({\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}})
and

(b.2)

:
z+\sum\{h^{\delta}_{s_{k}}(z^{\prime}_{k}):k<k(*)\}\in\varphi_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{\delta+1})
.

(c)

\langle{\mathbb{M}}_{\delta}\cup\{{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{s_{k}}:k<k(*)\}\rangle_{{\mathbb{M}}_{\delta+1}}\leq^{\rm pr}_{\varphi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\delta+1}
.

(d)

\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{\delta+1})=\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{\delta})+\sum\{\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{s}):s\in J_{\delta}\}
.

Proof.

(1). We proceed by induction on
\beta\geq\alpha
. The straightforward details leave to reader.

(2). If
\alpha\notin S,
then the conclusion follows from (1) and Lemma
3.18
. Now suppose that
\alpha\in S.
By Definition
3.32
(F), the result holds if
\beta=\alpha+1.
But then for any
\beta>\alpha
(and since
\alpha+1\notin S
),
we have

{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}\cap\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{\beta})={\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}\cap(\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{\beta})\cap{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha+1})=\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha+1})\cap{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}=\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}).

(3). Items (a), (c) and (d) can be proved easily by Definition
3.32
(F). Let us prove (b). To this end, take
z_{\ell}\in{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}}
for
\ell<k(*),z\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\delta}
,
and
z+\sum\{h^{\delta}_{s_{k}}(z_{k}):k<k(*)\}\in\varphi_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{\delta+1})
.

In the light of (c) we observe that

z+\sum\{h^{\delta}_{s_{k}}(z_{k}):k<k(*)\}\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}(\langle{\mathbb{M}}_{\delta}\cup\{{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{s_{k}}:k<k(*)\}\rangle_{{\mathbb{M}}_{\delta+1}}).

Let
k<k(*)
. We now apply the items
(a)
,
(c)
and
(d)

to find
y\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{\delta})

and
y_{k}\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{s_{k}})
such that

z+\sum\{h^{\delta}_{s_{k}}(z_{k}):k<k(*)\}=y+\sum\{y_{k}:k<k(*)\}.

Since
\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{s_{k}})
is the image of
\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}})
under
h_{s_{k}}^{\delta}
, we have

y_{k}=h_{s_{k}}^{\delta}(x_{k})
for some
x_{k}\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}}).

Then

\sum\{h^{\delta}_{s_{k}}(z_{k}-x_{k}):k<k(*)\}=y-z\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{\delta}).

As
s_{0},\cdots,s_{k(*)-1}
are chosen distinct, we deduce that

h^{\delta}_{s_{k}}(z_{k}-x_{k})\in\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h^{\delta}_{s_{k}})\cap\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{\delta})=\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}(\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h^{\delta}_{s_{k}})),

where
k<k(*).

Hence,
z_{k}-x_{k}\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}})
.

Let
h<\omega
be such that for each
k<k(*)
,

\begin{array}[]{ll}x_{k}&\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}\left(\bigoplus\limits_{\ell\leq h}(\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}))\right)\\
&=\bigoplus\limits_{\ell\leq h}\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}(\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell}))\\
&=\bigoplus\limits_{\ell<h}\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}(\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell}))\oplus\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}(\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{h})).\end{array}

Thus, for some
z^{\prime}_{k}\in\bigoplus\limits_{\ell<h}\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}(\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell}))
, we have

x_{k}-z^{\prime}_{k}\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}(\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf f}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{h}))\subseteq\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}}).

It then follows that

z_{k}-z^{\prime}_{k}=(z_{k}-x_{k})+(x_{k}-z^{\prime}_{k})\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}})+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}})=\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}}).

We now show that
w:=z+\sum\{h^{\delta}_{s_{k}}(z^{\prime}_{k}):k<k(*)\}\in\varphi_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{\delta+1}).
Indeed,

\begin{array}[]{ll}w&=z+\sum\{h^{\delta}_{s_{k}}(z^{\prime}_{k}-z_{k}):k<k(*)\}+\sum\{h^{\delta}_{s_{k}}(z_{k}):k<k(*)\}\\
&=(z+\sum\{h^{\delta}_{s_{k}}(z_{k}):k<k(*)\})+\sum\{h^{\delta}_{s_{k}}(z^{\prime}_{k}-z_{k}):k<k(*)\}\\
&\in\varphi_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{\delta+1})+\sum\{\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{P}}^{\delta}_{s_{k}}):k<k(*)\}\\
&=\varphi_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{\delta+1}).\end{array}

This completes the proof of (b) and hence of the lemma.
∎

The main result of this section is that under suitable assumptions on
(\lambda,{{\mathfrak{m}}},S,\bar{\gamma}^{*})
,
there is, in
\text{ZFC},
a semi-nice construction for it. Before, we state and prove our result, let us show that
under extra set theoretic assumptions we can get a stronger result.

Definition 3.35
.

Suppose
\lambda=\mathop{\mathrm{cf}}(\lambda)>|{\bf R}|+|{\bf S}|+\aleph_{0}

(2)

(3)

(4)

So in

,
{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}}\cap{\mathbb{M}}_{\delta}={\mathbb{N}}^{\ast}_{\delta}
.

Recall that for a stationary set
S\subseteq\lambda,
Jensen’s diamond
\diamondsuit_{\lambda}(S)
asserts the existence
of a sequence
\langle S_{\alpha}:\alpha\in S\rangle

such that for every
X\subseteq\lambda
the set
\{\alpha\in S:X\cap\alpha=S_{\alpha}\}

is stationary. It is easily seen that
\diamondsuit_{\lambda}(S)
implies
2^{<\lambda}=\lambda
. One the one hand, due to a well-known
theorem of Jensen,
\diamondsuit_{\lambda}(S)
holds in Gödel’s constructible universe
L
for all uncountable regular cardinals

\lambda
and all stationary sets
S\subseteq\lambda
. On the other hand, if
2^{<\lambda}
is forced to be above
\lambda,

then
\diamondsuit_{\lambda}(S)
fails for all stationary sets
S\subseteq\lambda
.
The next theorem of Shelah shows that under diamond principle, we can get very nicely

\langle{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}:\alpha\leq\lambda\rangle
.

Lemma 3.36
.

Suppose
{\mathfrak{m}}
is a non-trivial context,
\lambda=\mathop{\mathrm{cf}}(\lambda)>|{\bf R}|+|{\bf S}|+\kappa({\mathfrak{m}})+||{\mathfrak{m}}||
,
S\subseteq S^{\lambda}_{\aleph_{0}}
is a stationary non-reflecting subset of
\lambda
and
\diamondsuit_{\lambda}(S)
holds.
Then
there is a very nicely
construction for
(\lambda,{\mathfrak{m}},S)
such that

(1)

(\lambda,{\mathfrak{m}},S)
is strongly very nice.

(2)

If
\mathop{\mathrm{cf}}(\alpha)\in\kappa\smallsetminus S
and
\alpha<\beta\leq\lambda
, then
{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}
is a
{\mathcal{K}}
-
directed summand of
{\mathbb{M}}_{\beta}
.

Proof.

See
[
46
, Section 2]
.
∎

We would like to prove a similar result in
ZFC
, thus we have to avoid the use of diamond
in the above lemma. To this end, we use Shelah’s Black Box. This leads us to get a weaker conclusion than Lemma
3.36
,
but as we will see later, it is sufficient to get our desired results.

Notation 3.37
.

For the rest of this section we use the following:

•

\lambda>\kappa
be infinite cardinals.

•

\mathcal{H}_{<\theta}(X)
be the least set
Y
such that
Y\supseteq X
and if
y\subseteq Y

and
|y|<\theta
,then
y\in Y.

•

\langle\tau_{n}:n<\omega\rangle
be an increasing sequence of vocabularies, each of size
\leq\kappa
such that for each
n<\omega
,
there exists some unary predicate
P_{n}
in
\tau_{n+1}\setminus\tau_{n}
.

Definition 3.38
.

For
n<\omega
let
\mathcal{F}_{n}
be the family of sets of the form

\{(\mathcal{N}_{\ell},f_{\ell}):\ell\leq n\}

satisfying the following conditions:

(a)

f_{\ell}:\kappa^{\leq\ell}\to\lambda^{\leq\ell}
is a tree embedding, i.e.,

(1)

for each
\eta\in\kappa^{\leq\ell},~\eta
and
f_{\ell}(\eta)

have the same length,

(2)

for
\eta\lhd\nu
in
\kappa^{\leq\ell},~f_{\ell}(\eta)\lhd f_{\ell}(\nu)
,

(3)

~f_{\ell}
is one-to-one,

(b)

for
\ell+1\leq n,~f_{\ell+1}
extends
f_{\ell},

(c)

for some
\tau^{\prime}_{\ell}\subseteq\tau_{\ell},~\mathcal{N}_{\ell}
is a
\tau^{\prime}_{\ell}
-structure
of size
\leq\kappa
and the universe of
\mathcal{N}_{\ell}
, denoted by
N_{\ell}
, is a subset of

\mathcal{H}_{<\kappa^{+}}(\lambda)
.

(d)

\tau^{\prime}_{\ell+1}\cap\tau_{\ell}=\tau^{\prime}_{\ell}
and
\mathcal{N}_{\ell+1}\restriction\tau^{\prime}_{\ell}

extends
\mathcal{N}_{\ell}
.

(e)

if
P_{m}\in\tau^{\prime}_{m+1}
, then
P_{m}^{\mathcal{N}_{\ell}}=N_{\ell}.

(f)

if
x,y\in N_{\ell},
then
\{x,y\}\in N_{\ell}
and
\emptyset\in N_{\ell}.

(g)

\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(f_{\ell})\subseteq\mathcal{N}_{\ell}
.

Definition 3.39
.

Let
\langle\mathcal{F}_{n}:n<\omega\rangle
be as in Definition
3.38
. Then
\mathcal{F}_{\omega}
is the family consisting of all
pairs
(\mathcal{N},f)
such that for some sequence
\langle(\mathcal{N}_{\ell},f_{\ell}):\ell<\omega\rangle
, we have

(a)

for each
n<\omega
,
\{(\mathcal{N}_{\ell},f_{\ell}):\ell\leq n\}
belongs to
\mathcal{F}_{n},

(b)

f=\bigcup\limits_{\ell<\omega}f_{\ell}
and
\mathcal{N}=\bigcup\limits_{\ell<\omega}\mathcal{N}_{\ell}.

We may note that if for each
m<\omega,P_{m}\in\tau^{\prime}_{m+1}
, then for each

(\mathcal{N},f)\in\mathcal{F}_{\omega}
, there is a unique sequence
\langle(\mathcal{N}_{\ell},f_{\ell}):\ell<\omega\rangle

witnessing this.

Definition 3.40
.

Let
(\mathcal{N},f)\in\mathcal{F}_{\omega}
. A branch of
f
is any
\eta\in\lambda^{\omega}

such that for each
n<\omega,~\eta\restriction n\in\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(f)
. We use
\lim(f)
for the set of all branches of
f
.

Given
W\subseteq\mathcal{F}_{\omega}
, we define two games
\Game_{W}
and
\Game^{\prime}_{W}
as follows.

Definition 3.41
.

Suppose
W\subseteq\mathcal{F}_{\omega}
.

(1)

The game
\Game_{W}
of length
\omega
is defined as follows.
In the
n^{\rm th}
move, player I chooses
\mathcal{N}_{n}
such that
\{(\mathcal{N}_{\ell},f_{\ell}):\ell\leq n\}\in\mathcal{F}_{n}
(noting that
f_{0}
is determined),
and player II chooses a tree embedding
f_{n}:\kappa^{\leq n}\to\lambda^{\leq n}
extending

\bigcup\limits_{\ell<n}f_{\ell}
such that
\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(f_{n})\setminus(\bigcup\limits_{\ell<n}\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(f_{\ell}))

is disjoint to
\bigcup\limits_{\ell<n}N_{\ell}.

Player II wins if
(\bigcup\limits_{\ell<\omega}\mathcal{N}_{\ell},\bigcup\limits_{\ell<\omega}f_{\ell})\in W.

(2)

The game
\Game^{\prime}_{W}
of length
\omega
is defined as follows.
In the zero move, player I chooses
k<\omega
,
\{(\mathcal{N}_{\ell},f_{\ell}):\ell\leq k\}\in\mathcal{F}_{k}
, and
X_{0}\subseteq\lambda^{<\omega}
of size less than
\lambda
.
For
n>0,

in the
n^{\rm th}
move, player I chooses
\mathcal{N}_{k+n}
and
X_{n}
such that
\{(\mathcal{N}_{\ell},f_{\ell}):\ell\leq k+n\}\in\mathcal{F}_{k+n}

and
X_{n}\subseteq\lambda^{<\omega}
is of size less than
\lambda
.
Then player II chooses a tree embedding
f_{k+n}:\kappa^{\leq k+n}\to\lambda^{\leq k+n}
extending

\bigcup\limits_{\ell<k+n}f_{\ell}
such that
\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(f_{k+n})\setminus(\bigcup\limits_{\ell<k+n}\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(f_{\ell}))

is disjoint to
\bigcup\limits_{\ell<n}N_{\ell}\cup\bigcup\limits_{\ell<n}X_{\ell}.

Player II wins if
(\bigcup\limits_{\ell<\omega}\mathcal{N}_{\ell},\bigcup\limits_{\ell<\omega}f_{\ell})\in W.

Definition 3.42
.

Suppose
W\subseteq\mathcal{F}_{\omega}
. Recall that

(1)

W
is called a barrier if player I does not win
\Game_{W}
or even
\Game^{\prime}_{W}
.

(2)

W
is called a strong barrier if player II wins
\Game_{W}
and even
\Game^{\prime}_{W}
.

(3)

W
is called disjoint if for distinct
(\mathcal{N}^{1},f^{1}),(\mathcal{N}^{2},f^{2})
in
W
,
f^{1}
and
f^{2}
have
no common branch.

We are now ready to state the version of Shelah’s black box theorem that is needed
in this paper.

Lemma 3.43

(The Black Box theorem)
.

Suppose
\lambda>\kappa
are infinite cardinals,
\mathop{\mathrm{cf}}(\lambda)>\aleph_{0},~\lambda^{\aleph_{0}}=\lambda^{\kappa}

and
S\subseteq S^{\lambda}_{\aleph_{0}}
is stationary. Then there is a sequence

W=\{(\mathcal{N}^{\alpha},f^{\alpha}):\alpha<\alpha_{*}\}\subseteq\mathcal{F}_{\omega}

and a non-decreasing function

\zeta:\alpha_{*}\to S

such that the following properties are satisfied:

(1)

W
is a disjoint strong barrier,

(2)

every branch of
f^{\alpha}
is an increasing sequence converging to
\zeta(\alpha)\in S
,

(3)

each
\mathcal{N}^{\alpha}
is transitive,

(4)

if
\zeta(\beta)=\zeta(\alpha),\beta+\kappa^{\aleph_{0}}\leq\alpha<\alpha_{*}

and
\eta
is a branch of
f^{\alpha}
, then for some
k<\omega,\eta\restriction k\notin\mathcal{N}^{\beta},

(5)

if
\lambda=\lambda^{\kappa},
we can also demand that if
\eta
is a branch of
f^{\alpha}

and
\eta\restriction k\in\mathcal{N}^{\beta}
for all
k<\omega
, then
\mathcal{N}^{\alpha}\subseteq\mathcal{N}^{\beta}
,
and even for all
n<\omega,\mathcal{N}^{\alpha}_{n}\in\mathcal{N}^{\beta}.

Proof.

This is in
[
44
, 2.8]
.
∎

We now state and prove the main result of this section.

Theorem 3.44
.

Assume
{{\mathfrak{m}}}
is a non-trivial
context. Suppose
\lambda>||{\mathfrak{m}}||
is such that
\mathop{\mathrm{cf}}(\lambda)\geq\aleph_{1}+\kappa({\mathfrak{m}})
, for
\mu<\lambda,\mu^{\aleph_{0}}<\lambda
,

\kappa=\mathop{\mathrm{cf}}(\lambda)>\kappa({\mathfrak{m}})
and
S\subseteq S^{\kappa}_{\aleph_{0}}
is such that
S
and
S^{\kappa}_{\aleph_{0}}\setminus S

are stationary in
\kappa.
Then

(1)

There is a semi-nice construction
for
(\lambda,{\mathfrak{m}},S,\kappa)
.

(2)

If in addition
\lambda
is regular, then there is a nice construction
for
(\lambda,{\mathfrak{m}},S,\kappa)
.

(3)

In part (1) if we omit the assumption
\mathop{\mathrm{cf}}(\lambda)=\kappa
, and
let
\bar{\gamma}^{*}=\langle\gamma^{*}_{\alpha}:\alpha\leq\kappa\rangle

be such that
\gamma^{*}_{0}=||{\mathbb{M}}_{*}||
and for all
\alpha<\kappa

\gamma^{*}_{1+\alpha}=\gamma^{*}_{0}+\lambda\cdot\alpha
. Then there is a
semi-nice
construction for
(\lambda,{\mathfrak{m}},S,\bar{\gamma}^{*}).

(4)

In part (3) if
\kappa=\lambda
, then there is a
nice
construction for
(\lambda,{\mathfrak{m}},S,\bar{\gamma}^{*}).

Proof.

We prove (1) and (2). Clauses (3) and (4)
can be proved in a similar way. We start by fixing some notation and facts:

•

Without loss of generality for
{\mathfrak{e}}\in{\mathfrak{E}}
and
n<\omega
, the universe of

{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
is a cardinal.

•

As
\mathop{\mathrm{cf}}(\lambda)=\kappa,
let
\bar{\gamma}=\langle\gamma_{\alpha}^{0}:\alpha<\kappa\rangle
be
an increasing and continuous sequence
cofinal in
\lambda
.

•

Let
\bar{\gamma}^{\ast}=\langle\gamma^{\ast}_{\alpha}:\alpha\leq\kappa\rangle

be an increasing and continuous sequence of ordinals with limit
\lambda
such that

\gamma^{*}_{0}>||{\mathfrak{m}}||
and for each
\alpha<\kappa,\gamma^{*}_{\alpha+1}-\gamma^{*}_{\alpha}
is
>0
and divisible by

||{\mathfrak{m}}||^{\aleph_{0}}+\gamma^{\ast}_{\alpha}
. We further assume that for each
\alpha<\kappa,\gamma^{*}_{\alpha}\geq\gamma_{\alpha}.

•

Let
\langle{\mathbb{N}}^{*}_{\alpha}:\alpha<|\mathcal{K}|\rangle

be an enumeration of elements of
\mathcal{K}.

Set

\bar{S}:=\{\gamma^{\ast}_{\alpha}:\alpha\in S\}.

Then
\bar{S}
is a stationary subset of
\lambda
. For each
n<\omega
, let
\tau_{n}
be the countable vocabulary

\tau_{n}:=\{\in,F,G,P_{0},\cdots,P_{n-1},c_{0},c_{1},\cdots,c_{i},\cdots\}
, where
F,G
are unary function symbols,
P_{0},\cdots,P_{n-1}

are unary predicate symbols and
c_{i}
’s, for
i<\omega
are constant symbols. We now apply the black box theorem (see Lemma
3.43
)
to get a sequence

W=\{(\mathcal{N}^{\alpha},f^{\alpha}):\alpha<\alpha_{*}\}\subseteq\mathcal{F}_{\omega}

and a non-decreasing function

\zeta:\alpha_{*}\to\bar{S}.

By induction on
\epsilon\leq\kappa
we will construct

{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\epsilon}
which is going to be a semi-nice construction up to

\epsilon
in the following sense:

(a)

{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}
consists of

(a-1)

\langle{\mathbb{M}}_{\xi}:\xi\leq\epsilon\rangle
,

(a-2)

\langle{\mathbb{N}}_{t},h_{t}:t\in J_{\xi},\xi\in\epsilon\setminus S_{\epsilon}\rangle
,

(a-3)

S_{\epsilon}\subseteq S\cap\epsilon,

(a-4)

\mathcal{T}_{\epsilon},

(a-5)

\langle({\mathfrak{e}}_{s},{\mathbb{P}}_{s},\bar{\alpha}_{s},\bar{t}_{s},\bar{g}_{s},h_{s},q_{s}):s\in J_{\delta}
and
\delta\in S_{\epsilon}\rangle
,

(a-6)

\langle\gamma^{*}_{\xi}:\xi\leq\epsilon\rangle
.

(b)

{\mathcal{A}}

satisfies all the relevant parts of Definition
3.32
.

(c)

we have

(c-1)

if

\xi<\epsilon
, then
S_{\xi}=S_{\epsilon}\cap\xi
,

(c-2)

for
\delta\in S\cap\epsilon
,

J_{\delta}\subseteq\{\beta<\alpha_{*}:\zeta(\beta)=\gamma_{\delta}\},

(c-3)

if
\epsilon\notin S
, then

\epsilon\notin S_{\epsilon+1}
and

J_{\epsilon}=\{\beta:\exists\eta\in\mathcal{T}_{\epsilon},~\eta^{\frown}\langle\beta\rangle\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\epsilon+1}\}.

(d)

Let
\langle\gamma^{\prime}_{\epsilon}:\epsilon<\kappa\rangle
be an increasing and continuous sequence of ordinals cofinal in
\lambda

with
\gamma^{\prime}_{0}=\|{\mathbb{M}}^{{{\mathfrak{m}}}}_{*}\|
. The sequence is such that

(d-1)

\langle(n_{\gamma},{\mathfrak{e}}_{\gamma},f_{\gamma}):\gamma<\gamma^{\prime}_{\epsilon}\rangle
is a collection of triples
(n,{\mathfrak{e}},f)
where

n<\omega,{\mathfrak{e}}\in{\mathfrak{E}}
and
f
is a bimodule homomorphism from

{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
into
{\mathbb{M}}_{\epsilon}
,

(d-2)

every triple
(n,{\mathfrak{e}},f)
as above appears as some
(n_{\gamma},{\mathfrak{e}}_{\gamma},f_{\gamma}),

for some
\gamma<\gamma^{\prime}_{\epsilon}
, for some
\epsilon<\kappa
large enough.

(e)

we have

(e-1)

\langle{\mathcal{T}}_{\epsilon}:\epsilon<\kappa\rangle
is increasing and
continuous,

(e-2)

{\mathcal{T}}_{0}=\{<>\}
,

(e-3)

if
{\mathcal{T}}_{\epsilon+1}\setminus{\mathcal{T}}_{\epsilon}\neq\emptyset
,
then

{\mathcal{T}}_{\epsilon+1}=\{\eta^{\frown}\langle\beta\rangle:\eta\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\epsilon}\text{~and~}\beta\in[\gamma^{\prime}_{\epsilon},\gamma^{\prime}_{\epsilon+1})\}.

We now start defining
\mathcal{A}_{\epsilon}
for
\epsilon\leq\kappa.

\epsilon=0
. Set
{\mathbb{M}}_{0}={\mathbb{M}}_{*}
and let
\gamma^{*}_{0}
be the universe of
{\mathbb{M}}_{\ast}
.

\epsilon
is a limit ordinal.
Then set
{\mathbb{M}}_{\epsilon}=\bigcup\limits_{\xi<\epsilon}{\mathbb{M}}_{\xi}
, and for everything else, just take
the union of the previous ones.

\epsilon=\xi+1
and
\xi\notin S
. In the same vein as of Definition
3.32
(E), we define
{\mathbb{M}}_{\epsilon}
, so

{\mathbb{M}}_{\epsilon}={\mathbb{M}}_{\xi}\oplus\bigoplus\limits_{t\in J_{\xi}}{\mathbb{N}}_{t},

where
J_{\xi}
and
{\mathbb{N}}_{t}
, for
t\in J_{\xi}
are defined similar to Definition
3.32
(E). Everything else in the definition
of
\mathcal{A}_{\epsilon}
is clear how to define.

\epsilon=\delta+1
,
\delta\in S
and
\gamma^{*}_{\delta}\neq\gamma^{0}_{\delta}
. Then define
{\mathbb{M}}_{\epsilon}

as in the previous case (i.e., as in Definition
3.32
(E)).

Note that in all of the above cases, if
\delta<\epsilon
is in
S
,
s\in J_{\delta}
and in stage
\delta
the type
q_{s}=q^{\delta}_{s}

is defined, then in view of Lemma
2.13
(and its natural generalization to arbitrary direct sums),
q_{s}

continues to be omitted at
\epsilon
.

\epsilon=\delta+1
,
\delta\in S
and
\gamma^{*}_{\delta}=\gamma^{0}_{\delta}
.
This is the most important part of the induction which corresponds to the case (F) of Definition
3.32
. Set

\alpha^{*}_{\delta}=\mathop{\mathrm{sup}}\{\alpha+1:\zeta(\alpha)\leq\gamma^{0}_{\delta}\}.

By induction on
\alpha\leq\alpha^{*}_{\delta}
we define

•

{\mathbb{M}}_{\delta,\alpha}
,

•

w_{\delta,\alpha},

•

\eta_{\beta}
for
\beta\in w_{\delta,\alpha}
,

•

{\mathfrak{x}}_{\beta}=({\mathfrak{e}}_{\beta},{\mathbb{P}}_{\beta},\bar{\alpha}_{\beta},\bar{t}_{\beta},\bar{g}_{\beta},h_{\beta},q_{\beta})
and
\eta_{\beta}
for

\beta\in w_{\delta,\alpha}
,

such that:

(\alpha)

w_{\delta,\alpha}\subseteq\{\beta<\alpha:\zeta(\beta)=\gamma^{0}_{\delta}\}
and for
\beta<\alpha
,
w_{\delta,\alpha}\cap\beta=w_{\delta,\beta}.

(\beta)

\eta_{\beta}\in{\rm lim}(f^{\beta})
for
\beta\in w_{\delta,\alpha}
.

(\gamma)

clause (F) of Definition
3.32
is satisfied when we consider

(M_{\delta,\alpha},w_{\delta,\alpha},\langle{\mathfrak{x}}_{\beta}:\beta\in w_{\delta,\alpha}\rangle)

instead of

({\mathbb{M}}_{\delta+1},J_{\delta},\langle({\mathfrak{e}}_{s},{\mathbb{P}}_{s},\bar{\alpha}_{s},\bar{t}_{s},\bar{g}_{s},h_{s},q_{s}):s\in J_{\delta}\rangle).

\alpha=0
. Then set
{\mathbb{M}}_{\delta,0}={\mathbb{M}}_{\delta}
and
w_{\delta,0}=\emptyset
. There is nothing else to define.

\alpha
is a limit ordinal.
Then set
M_{\delta,\alpha}=\bigcup\limits_{\xi<\alpha}M_{\delta,\xi}
,

w_{\delta,\alpha}=\bigcup\limits_{\xi<\alpha}w_{\delta,\xi}
and for
\beta\in w_{\delta,\alpha}
pick some
\xi<\alpha

such that
\beta\in w_{\delta,\xi}
and then define
\eta_{\beta}
and
{\mathfrak{x}}_{\beta}
as defined at step

\xi
of the induction.

\alpha=\beta+1
and
\zeta(\beta)\neq\gamma^{0}_{\delta}
.
Then set
M_{\delta,\alpha}=M_{\delta,\beta}

and
w_{\delta,\alpha}=w_{\delta,\beta}
.

\alpha=\beta+1
and
\zeta(\beta)=\gamma^{0}_{\delta}
.
In this case we decide if we add
\beta
to our final
J_{\delta}

or not, where
J_{\delta}
is going to be
w_{\delta,\alpha^{*}_{\delta}}
. We consider two cases.

Case 1.
There are
i<2

and
\eta\in{\rm lim}(f^{\beta})

such that
({\mathbb{M}}_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,i},w_{\delta,\beta+1},\langle{\mathfrak{x}}_{\nu}:\nu\in w_{\delta,\beta+1}\rangle)

satisfies the conclusion of clause (F) of Definition
3.32
, where
({\mathbb{M}}_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,i},w_{\delta,\beta+1},\langle{\mathfrak{x}}_{\nu}:\nu\in w_{\delta,\beta+1}\rangle)

is defined as follows:

(
\delta
)

w_{\delta,\alpha}:=w_{\delta,\beta}\cup\{\beta\},

(
\epsilon
)

\eta_{\beta}:=\eta,

(
\varepsilon
)

{\mathfrak{x}}_{\beta}:=({\mathfrak{e}}_{\beta},{\mathbb{P}}_{\beta},\bar{\alpha}_{\beta},\bar{t}_{\beta},\bar{g}_{\beta},h_{\beta},q_{\beta})
, where this sequence is defined as follows:

(
\varepsilon
-1)

\bar{t}_{\beta}:=\langle t_{n}:n<\omega\rangle,
where for each
n<\omega,t_{n}=G^{\mathcal{N}^{\beta}}(\eta(n))
,

(
\varepsilon
-2)

for some
\alpha_{n}<\delta,t_{n}\in J_{\alpha_{n}}
(note that such an
\alpha_{n}
is unique if it exists),

(
\varepsilon
-3)

\bar{\alpha}_{\beta}:=\langle\alpha_{n}:n<\omega\rangle
is an increasing sequence of ordinals in
\delta\setminus S
cofinal in
\delta
,

(
\varepsilon
-4)

for each
n,m<\omega,\mathfrak{e}_{t_{n}}=\mathfrak{e}_{t_{m}}.
Set
\mathfrak{e}:=\mathfrak{e}_{t_{0}}
,

(
\varepsilon
-5)

\{x_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}},h_{t_{n}},t_{n},\alpha_{n}\}\in N_{n+1}^{\beta},

(
\varepsilon
-6)

\bar{g}_{\beta}:=\bar{g}_{\beta}^{\eta,i}:=\langle g_{\beta,n}^{\eta,i}:n<\omega\rangle
where
g_{n}=g_{\beta,n}^{\eta,i}:{\mathbb{N}}_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}\to{\mathbb{M}}_{\gamma_{\delta}^{**}},
for some
\gamma^{**}_{\delta}
, is defined by
g_{n}=i\cdot f_{c_{n}^{\mathcal{N}^{\beta}}}
,

(
\varepsilon
-7)

z_{\beta,n}^{\eta,i}:=F^{\mathcal{N}^{\beta}}\left(\sum\limits_{\ell<n}(g_{\ell}+h_{t_{\ell}})(x_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}})\right)
,

(
\varepsilon
-8)

q_{\beta}:=q_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,i}:=\{\varphi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}(y-z_{\beta,n}^{\eta,i}):n<\omega\}
,

(
\varepsilon
-9)

h_{\beta}:=h_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,i}:{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}}\to{\mathbb{P}}_{\beta}
is onto,

(
\varepsilon
-10)

{\mathbb{M}}_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,i}
is generated by
{\mathbb{M}}_{\delta,\beta}\cup{\mathbb{P}}_{\beta}
,

In this case we set

•

{\mathbb{M}}_{\delta,\beta+1}={\mathbb{M}}_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,i}
,

•

w_{\delta,\beta+1}=w_{\delta,\beta}\cup\{\beta\}
,

•

\eta_{\beta}=\eta,

•

for
\nu\in w_{\delta,\beta+1}
we let
\mathfrak{x}_{\nu}
be as above,

Case 2.
The above situation does not hold. Then we set
{\mathbb{M}}_{\delta,\beta+1}:={\mathbb{M}}_{\delta,\beta}

and
w_{\delta,\beta+1}:=w_{\delta,\beta}
.

Having considered the above cases, we set

•

{\mathbb{M}}_{\delta+1}:={\mathbb{M}}_{\delta,\alpha^{*}_{\delta}}
,

•

J_{\delta}:=w_{\delta,\alpha^{*}_{\delta}}
,

•

for
s\in J_{\delta}
,
\langle({\mathfrak{e}}_{s},{\mathbb{P}}_{s},\bar{\alpha}_{s},\bar{t}_{s},\bar{g}_{s},h_{s},q_{s}):s\in J_{\delta}\rangle
is defined to be
\mathfrak{x}_{s}.

This completes our inductive construction of
\mathcal{A}_{\epsilon}
’s for
\epsilon\leq\kappa.

Finally, we set
\mathcal{A}:=\mathcal{A}_{\kappa}
and
{\mathbb{M}}:={\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}.

We are going to show that
\mathcal{A}
is as required.

Clearly we have gotten a weakly semi-nice construction. We now show that it is indeed a semi-nice construction.
Let us prove the property presented in Definition
3.33
(3)
(G)_{1}
. Thus suppose that

{\mathfrak{e}}\in{\mathfrak{E}}
,
{\bf f}
is an endomorphism of
{\mathbb{M}}
as
an
{\bf R}
-module,
\gamma<\kappa
and for each
n<\omega
suppose
{\bf g_{n}}:{\mathbb{N}}_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}\to{\mathbb{M}}_{\gamma}

is a bimodule homomorphism. Suppose by contradiction that the property presented in Definition
3.33
(3)
(G)_{1}
fails. Let

{\bf g}:\mathcal{T}_{\kappa}\to\mathcal{T}_{\kappa},

where
\mathcal{T}_{\kappa}=\lambda^{<\omega}
, be a one-to-one order preserving (i.e,
\eta\lhd\nu
iff
{\bf g}(\eta)\lhd{\bf g}(\nu)
) embedding such that for each
\eta\in\mathcal{T}_{\kappa}\setminus\mathcal{T}_{0},\mathfrak{e}_{{\bf g}(\eta)}=\mathfrak{e}
.
Let

\mathcal{B}=\langle B,\in^{\mathcal{B}},F^{\mathcal{B}},G^{\mathcal{B}},(c_{n}^{\mathcal{B}})_{n<\omega},(P_{n}^{\mathcal{B}})_{n<\omega}\rangle

be a
\tau=\bigcup\limits_{n<\omega}\tau_{n}
-structure satisfying the following properties:

•

(\mathcal{B},\in^{\mathcal{B}})
expands
(\mathcal{H}_{<\aleph_{1}}({\mathbb{M}}),\in)
,

•

F^{\mathcal{B}}\restriction{\mathbb{M}}={\bf f},

•

G^{\mathcal{B}}={\bf g},

•

for
n<\omega,~c_{n}^{\mathcal{B}}=\gamma_{n}
, where
\gamma_{n}
is such that
(n,\mathfrak{e},{\bf g}_{n})=(n_{\gamma_{n}},\mathfrak{e}_{\gamma_{n}},f_{\gamma_{n}}).

Pick
\gamma^{**}\in\kappa\setminus S
such that
\gamma^{**}>\gamma
and for each

n<\omega,~{\bf f}({\bf g}_{n}(x_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}))\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\gamma^{**}}
. Recall that
\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf g}_{n})\subseteq{\mathbb{M}}_{\gamma}\subseteq{\mathbb{M}}_{\gamma^{**}}.

Note that the set

\{\delta\in S:\gamma^{*}_{\delta}=\gamma_{\delta}\}

is a stationary set. We are going to use the black box theorem.
In the light of Lemma
3.43
we observe that there are
\delta\in S
and
\beta\in J_{\delta}
such that

(1)

\zeta(\beta)=\gamma_{\delta},

(2)

\gamma^{*}_{\delta}=\gamma_{\delta},

(3)

\mathcal{N}^{\beta}\prec\mathcal{B},
in particular,

(a)

f^{\beta}={\bf f}\restriction\mathcal{N}^{\beta},

(b)

for all
n<\omega,c_{n}^{\mathcal{N}^{\beta}}=c_{n}^{\mathcal{B}}
,

(c)

for
n<\omega,{\bf g_{n}}=f_{c_{n}^{\mathcal{N}^{\beta}}}
.

Let
\epsilon=\delta+1
. Note that as
\delta\in S

and
\gamma^{*}_{\delta}=\gamma_{\delta}
, at step
\epsilon
of the construction we are
at one of the cases (1) or (2). The nontrivial part is to check the property presented in Definition
3.32
(n). If case (1) occurs we are immediately done. So suppose that we are in case (2), and hence, by the construction, we are in one of the following situations:

(\star)_{1}

for all
\eta\in\lim(f^{\beta})
and all
i<2
the type
q_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,i}
is realized in
{\mathbb{M}}_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,i}.

(\star)_{2}

there is some
\delta^{\prime}\in S_{\delta}
and some
\beta^{\prime}\in J_{\delta^{\prime}}
such that for some

\eta\in\lim(f^{\beta^{\prime}})
and some
i<2,q_{\delta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}^{\eta,i}
is well-defined and is omitted in
{\mathbb{M}}_{\delta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}^{\eta,i},
but it is realized in
{\mathbb{M}}_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,i}.

(\star)_{3}

there is some
\beta^{\prime}\in w_{\delta,\beta}
, some

\eta\in\lim(f^{\beta^{\prime}})
and some
i<2
such that
\beta^{\prime}\leq 2^{\aleph_{0}}+\beta
and
q_{\delta,\beta^{\prime}}^{\eta,i}
is well-defined and is omitted in
{\mathbb{M}}_{\delta,\beta^{\prime}}^{\eta,i},
but it is realized in
{\mathbb{M}}_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,i}.

We only consider the case
(\star)_{1}
and leave the other cases to the reader, as they are easier to prove. Pick some

\eta\in\lim(f^{\beta})
. For
i<2
the type

q_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,i}=\{\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}(y-z_{\beta,n}^{\eta,i}):n<\omega\}

is realized in
{\mathbb{M}}_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,i}
. By our construction,
{\mathbb{M}}_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,i}

is generated by
{\mathbb{M}}_{\delta,\beta}\cup{\mathbb{P}}_{\beta}
and
h_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,i}:{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}}\to{\mathbb{P}}_{\beta}

is onto, thus we can find
y_{i}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\delta,\beta}
and
z_{i}\in{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}}

such that
q_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,i}
is realized by

y_{i}+h_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,i}(z_{i})
. Hence for all
n<\omega
,

{\mathbb{M}}_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,i}\models\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}(y_{i}+h_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,i}(z_{i})-z_{\beta,n}^{\eta,i}).

Recall from (
\varepsilon
-6) that:

g_{\beta,n}^{\eta,i}=i\cdot f_{c_{n}^{\mathcal{N}^{\beta}}}=i\cdot{\bf g_{n}}\quad\quad\quad(\ast)

Hence

z_{\beta,n}^{\eta,i}=F^{\mathcal{N}^{\beta}}\left(\sum\limits_{\ell<n}(h_{t_{\ell}}+i\cdot{\bf g}_{\ell})(x_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}})\right)=\sum\limits_{\ell<n}{\bf f}(h_{t_{\ell}}(x_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}}))+i\cdot\sum\limits_{\ell<n}{\bf f}({\bf g}_{\ell}(x_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}})).

It follows for each
i<2
that

\begin{array}[]{ll}y_{i}+h_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,i}(z_{i})-z_{\beta,n}^{\eta,i}&=y_{i}+h_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,i}(z_{i})-\sum\limits_{\ell<n}{\bf f}(h_{t_{\ell}}(x_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}}))-i\cdot\sum\limits_{\ell<n}{\bf f}({\bf g}_{\ell}(x_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}}))\\
&\in\varphi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,i}).\\
\end{array}

By applying Lemma
3.34
(3) to
z_{0}\in{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}}

and
y_{0}-z_{\beta,n}^{\eta,0}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\delta,\beta}

we can find some
z^{\prime}_{n,0}\in\sum\limits_{\ell<\omega}\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf h}_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}})
equipped
with the following two properties:

(4)

z_{0}-z_{n,0}^{\prime}\in\varphi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}})
,

(5)

y_{0}-z_{\beta,n}^{\eta,0}+h_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,0}(z_{n,0}^{\prime})\in\varphi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{\delta,\beta})
.

In the same vein, there is

z_{n,1}^{\prime}\in\sum\limits_{\ell<\omega}\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf h}_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}})
such that

(6)

z_{1}-z_{n,1}^{\prime}\in\varphi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}})
,

(7)

y_{1}-z_{\beta,n}^{\eta,1}+h_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,1}(z_{n,1}^{\prime})\in\varphi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{\delta,\beta})
.

Given
n<\omega
, pick
k_{n}<\omega
such that

z_{n,0}^{\prime},z_{n,1}^{\prime}\in\sum\limits_{\ell<k_{n}}\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf h}_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}}).

So we can find
\{z^{\prime}_{n,0,\ell}:\ell<k_{n}\}
and
\{z^{\prime}_{n,1,\ell}:\ell<k_{n}\}
such that for
i<2,~z^{\prime}_{n,i,\ell}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf h}_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}})

and
z_{n,i}^{\prime}=\sum\limits_{\ell<k_{n}}z^{\prime}_{n,i,\ell}.
For
\ell<k_{n}
pick some
z_{n,i,\ell}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}
with

{\bf h}_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}}(z_{n,i,\ell})=z^{\prime}_{n,i,\ell}
.

Set
z=z_{1}-z_{0}.
In view of clauses (4) and (6) above, we have

z\in(z_{n,1}^{\prime}-z_{n,0}^{\prime})+\varphi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}})=\sum\limits_{\ell<k_{n}}{\bf h}_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}}(z_{n,1,\ell})-\sum\limits_{\ell<k_{n}}{\bf h}_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}}(z_{n,0,\ell})+\varphi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}})

for all
n
.
Thus part (a) of Definition
3.33
(3)
(G)_{1}
is satisfied.

For part (b) of Definition
3.33
(3)
(G)_{1}
, by items (5) and (7) we have

\sum\limits_{\ell<n}{\bf f}({\bf g}_{\ell}(x_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}}))\in(y_{1}-y_{0})+\left(h_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,1}(z^{\prime}_{n,1})-h_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,0}(z^{\prime}_{n,0})\right)+\varphi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{\delta,\beta})\quad(\dagger)

Now let
{\mathbb{K}}
be such that
{\mathbb{M}}_{\delta,\beta}={\mathbb{M}}_{\gamma^{**}}\oplus{\mathbb{K}}
and let
\pi:{\mathbb{M}}_{\delta,\beta}\to{\mathbb{K}}
be the natural projection. We apply
(\ast)
for
i=1
along with the property presented in Definition
3.32
(F)(k) to see:

(8)

h_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,1}(z_{n,1}^{\prime})=\sum\limits_{\ell<k_{n}}h_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,1}(z^{\prime}_{n,1,\ell})=\sum\limits_{\ell<k_{n}}h_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,1}({\bf h}_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}}(z_{n,1,\ell}))=\sum\limits_{\ell<k_{n}}(h_{t_{\ell}}+{\bf g}_{\ell})(z_{n,1,\ell})
.

Also, we apply
(\ast)
for
i=0
along with the property presented in Definition
3.32
(F)(k) to see:

(9)

h_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,0}(z_{n,0}^{\prime})=\sum\limits_{\ell<k_{n}}h_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,0}(z^{\prime}_{n,0,\ell})=\sum\limits_{\ell<k_{n}}h_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,1}({\bf h}_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}}(z_{n,0,\ell}))=\sum\limits_{\ell<k_{n}}h_{t_{\ell}}(z_{n,0,\ell}).

By the choice of
\gamma^{**}
,
{\bf f}({\bf g}_{\ell}(x_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}}))\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\gamma^{**}}
and
\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf g_{\ell}})\subseteq{\mathbb{M}}_{\gamma}\subseteq{\mathbb{M}}_{\gamma^{**}}
and hence the following assertions are valid as well:

(10)

for all
\ell<n,\pi({\bf f}({\bf g}_{\ell}(x_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}})))=0
,

(11)

for all
\ell<k_{n}
and
i<2,

\pi({\bf g}_{\ell}(z_{n,i,\ell}))=0
,

We may assume without loss of the generality that
\alpha_{\ell}>\gamma^{**}
for all
\ell<\omega
, where
\alpha_{\ell}
is such that
t_{\ell}\in J_{\alpha_{\ell}}
.
It then follows from Definition
3.32
(E) that for each
\ell<\omega,

\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h_{t_{\ell}})\subseteq{\mathbb{K}}

and hence:

(12)

for all
\ell<k_{n},\pi(h_{t_{\ell}}(z^{\prime}_{n,1,\ell}))=h_{t_{\ell}}(z^{\prime}_{n,1,\ell}).

By setting

(13)

y^{\prime}:=y_{1}-y_{0}
and
y:=y^{\prime}-\pi(y^{\prime})

in the previous formulas we observe that:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum\limits_{\ell<n}{\bf f}({\bf g}_{\ell}(x_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}}))&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(10)}}{{=}}\sum\limits_{\ell<n}{\bf f}({\bf g}_{\ell}(x_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}}))-\pi(\sum\limits_{\ell<n}{\bf f}({\bf g}_{\ell}(x_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}})))\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\dagger)}}{{\in}}(y_{1}-y_{0})+\left(h_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,1}(z^{\prime}_{n,1})-h_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,0}(z^{\prime}_{n,0})\right)+\varphi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{\delta,\beta}))\\
&\ \ -\pi(y_{1}-y_{0})-\pi\left(h_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,1}(z^{\prime}_{n,1})-h_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,0}(z^{\prime}_{n,0})\right)+\varphi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{\delta,\beta}))\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(13)}}{{=}}y+\left(h_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,1}(z^{\prime}_{n,1})-h_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,0}(z^{\prime}_{n,0})\right)-\pi\left(h_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,1}(z^{\prime}_{n,1})-h_{\delta,\beta}^{\eta,0}(z^{\prime}_{n,0})\right)+\varphi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{\delta,\beta})\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(8+9)}}{{=}}y+\sum\limits_{\ell<k_{n}}(h_{t_{\ell}}+{\bf g}_{\ell})(z_{n,1,\ell})-\sum\limits_{\ell<k_{n}}h_{t_{\ell}}(z_{n,0,\ell})\\
&\ \ -\pi\sum\limits_{\ell<k_{n}}(h_{t_{\ell}}+{\bf g}_{\ell})(z_{n,1,\ell})+\pi\sum\limits_{\ell<k_{n}}h_{t_{\ell}}(z_{n,0,\ell})+\varphi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{\delta,\beta})\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(11+12)}}{{=}}y+\sum\limits_{\ell<k_{n}}(h_{t_{\ell}}+{\bf g}_{\ell})(z_{n,1,\ell})-\sum\limits_{\ell<k_{n}}h_{t_{\ell}}(z_{n,0,\ell})\\
&\ \ -\sum\limits_{\ell<k_{n}}(h_{t_{\ell}})(z_{n,1,\ell})+\sum\limits_{\ell<k_{n}}h_{t_{\ell}}(z_{n,0,\ell})+\varphi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{\delta,\beta})\\
&=y+\sum\limits_{\ell<k_{n}}{\bf g}_{\ell}(z_{n,1,\ell})+\varphi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{\delta,\beta}).\end{array}

This completes the proof of Definition
3.33
(3)
(G)_{1}
(b).

We now sketch the proof of property
(G)_{2}
of Definition
3.33
(3), as its details are similar to the above. Let
{\mathfrak{e}}\in{\mathfrak{E}}^{\mathfrak{m}}
and
for
n<\omega
and
\alpha\in\kappa\smallsetminus S
.
By
{\bf h}_{\alpha,n}

we mean an embedding of
{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
into

{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}

such that
{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}+\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf h}_{\alpha,n})={\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}\oplus\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf h}_{\alpha,n})\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
.

We look at the following set

C:=\{\delta<\kappa:\forall\alpha<\delta~\forall n<\omega,\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf h}_{\alpha,n})\subseteq{\mathbb{M}}_{\delta}\},

which
C
is a club subset of
\kappa
.
11
11

11

Club means closed and unbounded.

Let

{\bf g}:\mathcal{T}_{\kappa}\to\mathcal{T}_{\kappa}

be such that for all
\eta,\mu\in\lim({\bf g})
and all
n<\omega,{\bf g}(\eta(n))={\bf g}(\mu(n))
.
Let

\mathcal{B}=\langle B,\in^{\mathcal{B}},F^{\mathcal{B}},G^{\mathcal{B}},(c_{n}^{\mathcal{B}})_{n<\omega},(P_{n}^{\mathcal{B}})_{n<\omega}\rangle

be a
\tau=\bigcup\limits_{n<\omega}\tau_{n}
-structure satisfying the following properties:

•

(\mathcal{B},\in^{\mathcal{B}})
expands
(\mathcal{H}_{<\aleph_{1}}({\mathbb{M}}),\in)
,

•

G^{\mathcal{B}}={\bf g},

•

for
n<\omega,~c_{n}^{\mathcal{B}}=\gamma_{n}
, where
\gamma_{n}
is such that
(n,\mathfrak{e},{\bf h}_{\alpha_{n},n}-h_{t_{n}})=(n_{\gamma_{n}},\mathfrak{e}_{\gamma_{n}},f_{\gamma_{n}}),
where
t_{n}={\bf g}(\eta(n))
and
\alpha_{n}
is such that
t_{n}\in J_{\alpha_{n}}
.

In the same vein, we can find
\delta\in S\cap C
,
\beta\in J_{\delta}
such that

(1)

\zeta(\beta)=\gamma_{\delta},

(2)

\gamma^{*}_{\delta}=\gamma_{\delta},

(3)

\mathcal{N}^{\beta}\prec\mathcal{B},
in particular,

(a)

f^{\beta}={\bf f}\restriction\mathcal{N}^{\beta},

(b)

for all
n<\omega,c_{n}^{\mathcal{N}^{\beta}}=c_{n}^{\mathcal{B}}
,

(c)

for
n<\omega,{\bf h}_{\alpha_{n},n}-h_{t_{n}}=f_{c_{n}^{\mathcal{N}^{\beta}}}
, where
t_{n}
and
\alpha_{n}
are defined as above.

(4)

The properties presented in Clause (F) of Definition
3.32
are hold.

According to Definition
3.32
(F)(k), we have

{\bf h_{\beta}^{\delta}}\circ{\bf h}_{n}=h_{t_{n}}+({\bf h}_{\alpha_{n},n}-h_{t_{n}})={\bf h}_{\alpha_{n},n},

as claimed by
(G)_{2}
from Definition
3.33
(3).

In order to prove clause (2) of the theorem,
suppose further that
\lambda
is a regular cardinal. We show that by shrinking
S
, if necessary, we can assume that the above constructed structure is indeed a nice construction, i.e., we can omit “semi” from it. The map
\delta\mapsto\gamma^{**}_{\delta}
is a regressive function on
S
. We are going to use the Fodor’s lemma. This says that
\delta\mapsto\gamma^{**}_{\delta}
is constant on some stationary subset
S_{1}
of
S
, thus for some
\gamma^{**}
and for all
\delta\in S_{1},\gamma^{**}_{\delta}=\gamma^{**}
. Now note that for each
\delta\in S_{1}
and each

s\in J_{\delta},\mathfrak{e}^{\delta}_{s}
is of the form
\mathfrak{e}_{\beta}
for some
\beta<\gamma^{**}
,
so again by Odor’s lemma
and for each
s\in J_{\delta},\bar{g}^{\delta}_{s}
is an
\omega
-sequence
\langle g^{\delta}_{s,n}:n<\omega\rangle

where for each
n,g^{\delta}_{s,n}:{\mathbb{N}}_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}_{s}}\to{\mathbb{M}}_{\gamma^{**}}
is a bimodule homomorphism.
But, the set

\{g:g:{\mathbb{N}}_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}_{s}}\to{\mathbb{M}}_{\gamma^{**}}\text{~is a bimodule homomorphism}\}

has size less than
\lambda
.
Now given an
\delta\in S_{1}
for which clause (F) of Definition
3.32
holds, the sets

\{\mathfrak{e}_{s}:s\in J_{\delta}\}~~\&~~\{\bar{g}^{\delta}_{s}:s\in J_{\delta}\}

have size less than
\lambda
. By another application of Fodor’s lemma, we can find a stationary subset
S_{2}

of
S_{1}
such that the sets
\{\mathfrak{e}_{s}:s\in J_{\delta}\}
and
\{\bar{g}^{\delta}_{s}:s\in J_{\delta}\}
are the same for all
\delta\in S_{2}.

This concludes the required result.
∎

4.
Any endomorphism is somewhat definable

In this section we shall investigate what “
\bar{\mathbb{M}}=\langle{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}:\alpha\leq\kappa\rangle
is a semi-nice construction”
gives us and look somewhat at various implications.

Definition 4.1
.

Given
\alpha<\kappa
,

n<\omega
and
{\mathbf{f}}:{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}\to{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
a bimodule homomorphism the notation

({\mathord{\mathrm{Pr}}})^{n}_{\alpha}{[}{\mathbf{f}},{\mathfrak{e}}{]}

stands for the following statement: if
{\bf h}:{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}\to{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
is a bimodule homomorphism,
then

{\bf f}({\bf h}(x_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}))\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa})+\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf h}),

for all
\ell<\omega
.

As an application of the results of Section 3, we present the following statement. This plays an essential role in proving some stronger versions of
({\mathord{\mathrm{Pr}}})^{n}_{\alpha}{[}{\mathbf{f}},{\mathfrak{e}}{]}
, see blow.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
{\mathcal{A}}

be a semi-nice construction for
(\lambda,{{\mathfrak{m}}},S,\kappa)
and suppose
{\bf f}:{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}\to{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
is an endomorphism of

{\bf R}
-modules and suppose
\kappa\geq\kappa({\mathfrak{E}})
.
Then for every
{\mathfrak{e}}\in{\mathfrak{E}}

there are
\alpha\in\kappa\setminus S
and

n(\ast)<\omega
such that

({\mathord{\mathrm{Pr}}})^{n(\ast)}_{\alpha}{[}{\mathbf{f}},{\mathfrak{e}}{]}
holds.

Proof.

Assume not. Then for every
\epsilon\in\kappa\setminus S
and
n<\omega

we can find a bimodule homomorphism
{\bf h}_{\epsilon,n}:{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}\to{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
such that for some
\ell(\epsilon,n)<\omega
,

{\bf f}({\bf h}_{\epsilon,n}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}))\notin{\mathbb{M}}_{\epsilon}+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell(\epsilon,n)}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa})+\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf h}_{\epsilon,n}).

Without loss of generality, we may assume that

n<\ell(\epsilon,n).
The following set

E:=\{\zeta<\kappa:\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf f}\upharpoonright{\mathbb{M}}_{\zeta})\subseteq{\mathbb{M}}_{\zeta}\text{~and~}\forall\xi<\zeta~\forall n<\omega,\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf h}_{\xi,n})\subseteq{\mathbb{M}}_{\zeta}\}

is a club subset of
\kappa
. Since
S

is stationary, we have

S\cap\lim\left(E\cap(S^{\kappa}_{\aleph_{0}}\setminus S)\right)\neq\emptyset,

where
\lim
denote the set of limit points. Let
\zeta
be in this intersection.
Then
\mathop{\mathrm{cf}}(\zeta)=\aleph_{0}
and there exists an increasing sequence
\langle\zeta_{n}:0<n<\omega\rangle
of elements
of
E\cap(S^{\kappa}_{\aleph_{0}}\setminus S)
cofinal in
\zeta
.

For each
0<\ell<\omega,
as
\zeta_{\ell}\notin S,
we can find a bimodule
{\mathbb{K}}_{\ell}
such that
{\mathbb{M}}_{\zeta_{\ell+1}}={\mathbb{M}}_{\zeta_{\ell}}\oplus{\mathbb{K}}_{\ell}
. Set also

{\mathbb{K}}_{0}={\mathbb{M}}_{\zeta_{0}}
. Thus,
{\mathbb{M}}_{\zeta}=\bigcup\limits_{\ell<\omega}{\mathbb{M}}_{\zeta_{\ell}}=\bigoplus\limits_{\ell<\omega}{\mathbb{K}}_{\ell}.

Let
{\bf g}^{*}_{\ell}:{\mathbb{M}}_{\zeta_{\ell+1}}\to{\mathbb{K}}_{\ell}
be the natural projection, this is well-defined, because
{\mathbb{K}}_{\ell}
is a direct summand of
{\mathbb{M}}_{\zeta_{\ell+1}}
.
Pick also an infinite subset
\mathcal{U}\subseteq\omega
such that

\forall n,m\in\mathcal{U}\left(n<m\implies\ell(\zeta_{n},n)<m\right).

For each
n<\omega
, we set
t_{n}:={\bf h}_{\zeta_{n},n}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n})\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\zeta}
. By Definition
3.33
(3)
(G)_{1}
,
we can find
y\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa},~z\in{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}}

and a sequence
\{z_{n,\ell},z^{\prime}_{n,\ell}:n\in\mathcal{U},\ell<\omega\}
with
z_{n,\ell}\in{\rm Dom\/}({\bf h}_{\zeta_{\ell},\ell})
such that for each
n\in\mathcal{U}
,
z_{n,\ell}=0
for all large enough
\ell
, say for all
\ell\geq k_{n}

and for all large enough
n\in\mathcal{U}
we have

(a)

z\in\sum\limits_{\ell\in\mathcal{U}\cap k_{n}}{\bf h}_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}}(z^{\prime}_{n,\ell})+\varphi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}}).

(b)

\sum\limits_{\ell\in\mathcal{U}\cap n}{\bf f}({\bf h}_{\zeta_{\ell},\ell}(x_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}}))\in y+\sum\limits_{\ell\in\mathcal{U}\cap k_{n}}{\bf h}_{\zeta_{\ell},\ell}(z_{n,\ell})+\varphi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}).

As
\zeta\notin S,{\mathbb{M}}_{\zeta}\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
and hence for some
{\mathbb{K}}
we have
y\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\zeta}\oplus{\mathbb{K}}

and
{\mathbb{M}}_{\zeta}\oplus{\mathbb{K}}\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
.
So, in clause
(b)
all elements are in
{\mathbb{M}}_{\zeta_{n}}\oplus{\mathbb{K}}
. This allow us to replace

\varphi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa})
with
\varphi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{\zeta_{n}}\oplus{\mathbb{K}})

to get the following new clause:

(b^{\prime})_{n}

:

\sum\limits_{\ell\in\mathcal{U}\cap n}{\bf f}({\bf h}_{\zeta_{\ell},\ell}(x_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}}))\in y+\sum\limits_{\ell\in\mathcal{U}\cap k_{n}}{\bf h}_{\zeta_{\ell},\ell}(z_{n,\ell})+\varphi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{\zeta_{n}}\oplus{\mathbb{K}}).

Let
\pi:{\mathbb{M}}_{\zeta}\oplus{\mathbb{K}}\to{\mathbb{M}}_{\zeta}
be the natural projection and let
y^{\prime}:=\pi(y)
. Recall that

\pi(\varphi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{\zeta_{n}}\oplus{\mathbb{K}}))=\varphi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{\zeta_{n}})
. By applying
\pi

along with
(b^{\prime})_{n}
we lead to the following equation:

(b^{\prime\prime})_{n}

:

\sum\limits_{\ell\in\mathcal{U}\cap n}{\bf f}({\bf h}_{\zeta_{\ell},\ell}(x_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}}))\in y^{\prime}+\sum\limits_{\ell\in\mathcal{U}\cap k_{n}}{\bf h}_{\zeta_{\ell},\ell}(z_{n,\ell})+\varphi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{\zeta_{n}}).

Pick
n(*)<\omega
such that
y^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\zeta_{n(*)}}
and choose successive elements
n<m
in
\mathcal{U}
bigger than
n(*)
which are large enough. Pick
w\in\varphi_{m}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{\zeta_{m}})
such that

(1)

\sum\limits_{\ell\in\mathcal{U}\cap m}{\bf f}({\bf h}_{\zeta_{\ell},\ell}(x_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}}))=y^{\prime}+\sum\limits_{\ell\in\mathcal{U}\cap k_{m}}{\bf h}_{\zeta_{\ell},\ell}(z_{m,\ell})+w.

Then we have:

(2)

As
\sum\limits_{\ell\in\mathcal{U}\cap n}{\bf f}({\bf h}_{\zeta_{\ell},\ell}(x_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}})),y^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\zeta_{n}}
, by (1) we have

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\bf g}^{*}_{n}({\bf h}_{\zeta_{n},n}(x_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}))&={\bf g}^{*}_{n}\left(\sum\limits_{\ell\in\mathcal{U}\cap m}{\bf f}({\bf h}_{\zeta_{\ell},\ell}(x_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}}))\right)\\
&=\sum\limits_{\ell\in\mathcal{U}\cap k_{m}}{\bf g}^{*}_{n}\left({\bf h}_{\zeta_{\ell},\ell}(z_{m,\ell})\right)+{\bf g}^{*}_{n}(w).\end{array}

In particular, we drive the following:

(3)

\sum\limits_{\ell\in\mathcal{U}\cap k_{m}}{\bf g}^{*}_{n}\left({\bf h}_{\zeta_{\ell},\ell}(z_{m,\ell})\right)\in\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf g}^{*}_{n}\circ{\bf h}_{\zeta_{n},n})+\varphi_{m}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{\zeta_{m}}).

Due to the definition of
{\bf g}^{*}_{n}
we know:

(4)

{\bf f}({\bf h}_{\zeta_{n},n}(x_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}))-{\bf g}^{*}_{n}\left({\bf f}({\bf h}_{\zeta_{n},n}(x_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}))\right)\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\zeta_{n}}.

Putting all things together we have

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\bf f}({\bf h}_{\zeta_{n},n}(x_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}))&\in{\bf g}_{n}^{*}\left({\bf f}({\bf h}_{\zeta_{n},n}(x_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}))\right)+{\mathbb{M}}_{\zeta_{n}}\\
&\subseteq\sum\limits_{\ell\in\mathcal{U}\cap k_{m}}{\bf g}_{n}^{*}\left({\bf h}_{\zeta_{\ell},\ell}(z_{m,\ell})\right)+{\mathbb{M}}_{\zeta_{n}}+\varphi_{m}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{\zeta_{m}})\\
&\subseteq{\mathbb{M}}_{\zeta_{n}}+\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf g}^{*}_{n}\circ{\bf h}_{\zeta_{n},n})+\varphi_{m}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{\zeta_{m}})\\
&={\mathbb{M}}_{\zeta_{n}}+\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf h}_{\zeta_{n},n})+\varphi_{m}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{\zeta_{m}}).\end{array}

This is a contradiction and the lemma follows.
∎

Definition 4.3
.

By
\text{\bf hds}^{{\mathbb{M}}_{2}}_{{\mathbb{M}}_{1}}(h,{\mathbb{N}})
we mean the following data:

(1)

{\mathbb{M}}_{1},{\mathbb{M}}_{2},{\mathbb{N}}
are bimodules,

(2)

{\mathbb{M}}_{1}\subseteq{\mathbb{M}}_{2}
,

(3)

h
is a bimodule homomorphism from
{\mathbb{N}}
into

{\mathbb{M}}_{2}
,

(4)

{\mathbb{N}}\in c\ell_{is}({\mathcal{K}})
,

(5)

{\mathbb{M}}_{1}+\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h)={\mathbb{M}}_{1}\oplus\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h)
,

(6)

{\mathbb{M}}_{1}+\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h)\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{2}
.

Definition 4.4
.

Let
{\mathbf{f}}:{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}\to{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
be an
{\bf R}
-module homomorphism. By
({\mathord{\mathrm{Pr}}}^{-})^{n}_{\alpha}[{\mathbf{f}},{\mathfrak{e}}]
we mean the following statement:
if
\text{\bf hds}^{{\mathbb{M}}_{\beta}}_{{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}}(h,{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n})

where

\alpha<\beta\in\kappa\setminus S
, then

{\mathbf{f}}(h(x_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}))\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}+\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h)+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}),

for all
\ell<\omega
.

The next lemma summarizes the main properties of the above defined statement.

Lemma 4.5
.

(1)

If
\text{\bf hds}^{{\mathbb{M}}_{2}}_{{\mathbb{M}}_{1}}(h_{1},{\mathbb{N}})
holds,
h_{0}
is a
bimodule homomorphism
from
{\mathbb{N}}
into
{\mathbb{M}}_{1}
and
h=h_{0}+h_{1}
, then

\text{\bf hds}^{{\mathbb{M}}_{2}}_{{\mathbb{M}}_{1}}(h,{\mathbb{N}})
holds as well.

(2)

If
{\mathbb{M}}_{0}\subseteq{\mathbb{M}}_{1}\subseteq{\mathbb{M}}_{2}
are
bimodules,
{\mathbb{M}}_{0}\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{1}

and
\text{\bf hds}^{{\mathbb{M}}_{2}}_{{\mathbb{M}}_{1}}(h,{\mathbb{N}})
holds, then so does
\text{\bf hds}^{{\mathbb{M}}_{2}}_{{\mathbb{M}}_{0}}(h,{\mathbb{N}})
.

(3)

Assume
\alpha\leq\beta<\kappa
,
\alpha\notin S
and

\beta\notin S
.
Then
({\mathord{\mathrm{Pr}}}^{-})^{n(\ast)}_{\alpha}[{\mathbf{f}},{\mathfrak{e}}]
implies

({\mathord{\mathrm{Pr}}}^{-})^{n(\ast)}_{\beta}[{\mathbf{f}},{\mathfrak{e}}]
.

(4)

If
({\mathord{\mathrm{Pr}}})^{n(\ast)}_{\alpha}[{\bf f},{\mathfrak{e}}]
holds, then

({\mathord{\mathrm{Pr}}}^{-})^{n(\ast)}_{\alpha}[{\bf f},{\mathfrak{e}}]
holds too.

(5)

Assume
\bar{{\mathbb{M}}}=\langle{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}:\alpha\leq\kappa\rangle
is a weakly semi-nice
construction for
(\lambda,{\mathfrak{m}},S,\kappa)
. If

\text{\bf hds}^{{\mathbb{M}}_{\lambda}}_{{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}}(h,{\mathbb{N}})
holds where

\alpha<\kappa
, then for a club of

\beta\in(\alpha,\kappa)
, also

\text{\bf hds}^{{\mathbb{M}}_{\beta}}_{{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}}(h,{\mathbb{N}})
holds.

(6)

If
\text{\bf hds}^{{\mathbb{M}}_{2}}_{{\mathbb{M}}_{1}}(h,{\mathbb{N}})
holds, then so does

\text{\bf hds}^{{\mathbb{M}}_{1}+\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h)}_{{\mathbb{M}}_{1}}(h,{\mathbb{N}})
.

Proof.

(1)

We only need to show that
{\mathbb{M}}_{1}+\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h)={\mathbb{M}}_{1}\oplus\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h)\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{2}
. But, this is clear as

{\mathbb{M}}_{1}+\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h)={\mathbb{M}}_{1}+\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h_{1})
and the property
\text{\bf hds}^{{\mathbb{M}}_{2}}_{{\mathbb{M}}_{1}}(h_{1},{\mathbb{N}})
holds.

(2)

We have

{\mathbb{M}}_{0}+\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h)={\mathbb{M}}_{0}\oplus\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h)\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{1}\oplus\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h)\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{2},

from which the result follows.

(3)

This is a combination of Lemma
3.34
(1) and clause (2) above.

(4)

Clear.

(5)

By our assumption,
h:{\mathbb{N}}\to{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
is a bimodule homomorphism and
{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}+\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h)={\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}\oplus\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h)\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}.
But then for a club
C\subseteq(\alpha,\kappa)
and for all
\beta\in C,{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}\oplus\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h)\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\beta}.
Then
C
is as required.

(6)

Clear from the definition.

∎

Definition 4.6
.

Let
{\mathbf{f}}:{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}\to{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
be a bimodule homomorphism,

\alpha<\kappa,~n<\omega
and let
z\in{\mathbb{N}}_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}
. The notation
({\mathord{\mathrm{Pr}}}1)^{n}_{\alpha,z}{[{\bf f},{\mathfrak{e}}]}
stands for the following statement: if
h
is a bimodule homomorphism
from
{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
into
{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
, then

{\bf f}(h(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}))-h(z)\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}).

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
\langle{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}:\alpha\leq\kappa\rangle

be a weakly
semi-nice
construction for
(\lambda,{{\mathfrak{m}}},S,\kappa)
and

{\bf f}:{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}\to{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
be an
{\bf R}
-endomorphism and let
\alpha\notin S
be such that
({\mathord{\mathrm{Pr}}}^{-})^{n(\ast)}_{\alpha}[{\bf f},{\mathfrak{e}}]
holds. Then
the property
({\mathord{\mathrm{Pr}}}1)^{n(\ast)}_{\alpha,z}{[{\bf f},{\mathfrak{e}}]}
holds for some

z\in{\mathbb{L}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)}[{\mathcal{K}}^{\mathfrak{m}}]
.

Proof.

Let
x:=x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(*)}
.
We shall prove the lemma in a sequence of claims, see Claims
4.8
–
4.11
.

Claim 4.8
.

Let
\beta\in(\alpha,\lambda)\setminus S
be such that

\text{\bf{hds}}^{{\mathbb{M}}_{\beta}}_{{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}}(h,{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)})
holds. Then

{\mathbf{f}}(h(x))\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}+\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h)+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}).

Proof.

Let
{\mathbb{N}}:=\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h)
. We know that

{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}+{\mathbb{N}}={\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\beta}
and

{\mathbb{M}}_{\beta}\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
. Combining these yields that

{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
.
In view of Definition
3.15
and Lemma
3.20
(6), there is
{\mathbb{K}}\subseteq{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}

such that

({\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}\oplus{\mathbb{N}})+{\mathbb{K}}={\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}\oplus{\mathbb{K}}\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}.

Then, we may assume that
{\mathbf{f}}(h(x))\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}\oplus{\mathbb{K}}
. Since

{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}\oplus{\mathbb{K}}\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
, and in the light of Lemma
3.18

we observe that
{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}\oplus{\mathbb{K}}\leq^{pr}_{\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
holds for all
n<\omega
. According to
the property
({\mathord{\mathrm{Pr}}}^{-})^{n(*)}_{\alpha}[{\bf f},{\mathfrak{e}}]
, we know

{\bf f}(h(x))\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}+{\mathbb{N}}+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}),

for all
n<\omega.
We use these to find elements
x_{1,n}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}
and
x_{2,n}\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that

{\mathbf{f}}(h(x))-x_{1,n}-x_{2,n}\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}).

It follows from

{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}\oplus{\mathbb{K}}\leq^{pr}_{\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
that

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\bf f}(h(x))&=x_{1,n}+x_{2,n}+({\mathbf{f}}(h(x))-x_{1,n}-x_{2,n})\\
&\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}+{\mathbb{N}}+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}\oplus{\mathbb{K}}).\end{array}

Let
g
be the projection from
{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}\oplus{\mathbb{K}}
onto

{\mathbb{K}}
. Recall that its kernel is
{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}
.
Let
z^{1}_{n}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}
,

z^{2}_{n}\in{\mathbb{N}}
and

z^{3}_{n}\in\varphi_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}\oplus{\mathbb{K}})
be such that

{\bf f}(h(x))=z^{1}_{n}+z^{2}_{n}+z^{3}_{n}
. Let us evaluate
g
on both sides of this formula to obtain:

g({\bf f}(h(x)))=g(z^{1}_{n})+g(z^{2}_{n})+g(z^{3}_{n})=z^{3}_{n}\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}\oplus{\mathbb{K}})\subseteq\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}).

As this holds for each
n
, we have

g({\bf f}(h(x)))\in\bigcap\limits_{n<\omega}\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}).

So,

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\bf f}(h(x))&=\left({\bf f}(h(x))-g({\bf f}(h(x)))\right)+(g({\bf f}(h(x)))\\
&\in({\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}\oplus{\mathbb{N}})+\bigcap_{n<\omega}\varphi_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa})\\
&=\quad{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}+\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h)+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}).\end{array}

The claim follows.
∎

Claim 4.9
.

For
i=1,2
, let
{\mathbb{N}}^{\ast}_{i}
and
\beta_{i}
be such that
{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}^{\ast}_{i}\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\beta_{i}}
and
\alpha<\beta_{i}<\kappa
where
\beta_{i}

is not in
S
. Let
h_{i}:{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)}\to{\mathbb{N}}^{\ast}_{i}
be an
isomorphism and
z_{i}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)}
be such that

{\bf f}(h_{i}(x))-h_{i}(z_{i})\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}).

Then

z_{1}\equiv z_{2}\ \mod\ \varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)})
.

Proof.

Let
\beta\in\kappa\setminus S
be large enough such that
\beta>\beta_{1}
and

\beta>\beta_{2}
.
Let
{\mathbb{N}}^{\ast}_{3}
be isomorphic to
{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)}
and
such that for any large enough
\gamma<\kappa
,

{\mathbb{M}}_{\beta}+{\mathbb{N}}^{\ast}_{3}={\mathbb{M}}_{\beta}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}^{\ast}_{3}\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\gamma}.

Let
h_{3}
be an isomorphism from
{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)}
onto

{\mathbb{N}}^{\ast}_{3}
. In the light of Claim
4.8
we deduce that

{\bf f}(h_{3}(x))-h_{3}(z_{3})\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}),

where

z_{3}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)}
.
By the transitivity of the equivalence relation, it is enough to show that
z_{3}\equiv z_{1}
and
z_{3}\equiv z_{2}\ \mod\ \varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)})

in
{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)}
; and by the
symmetry it is enough to prove
z_{3}\equiv z_{1}

\mod\ \varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)})
.

Pick
\gamma>\beta
large enough. Then

{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}^{\ast}_{1}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}^{\ast}_{3}\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\gamma}.

Let
{\mathbb{N}}^{\ast}_{4}=\{h_{1}(z)-h_{3}(z):z\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)}\}
and
define
h_{4}:{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{M}}_{\beta+1}
by

h_{4}(z)=h_{1}(z)-h_{3}(z).

Due to its definition, we know
{\mathbb{N}}^{\ast}_{4}
is a sub-bimodule of
{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}^{*}_{1}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}^{*}_{3}

and hence of
{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
. Also,

h_{4}
is an isomorphism from
{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)}
onto

{\mathbb{N}}^{\ast}_{4}
, and
{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}^{\ast}_{1}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}^{\ast}_{3}={\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}^{\ast}_{1}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}^{\ast}_{4}
.
