Thus, we have

(\ast)_{1}

{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}^{\ast}_{4}\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}^{\ast}_{1}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}^{\ast}_{3}\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\gamma}
.

Now modulo
{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa})
, we have

(\ast)_{2}

{\bf f}(h_{4}(x))={\bf f}(h_{1}(x)-h_{3}(x))={\bf f}(h_{1}(x))-{\bf f}(h_{3}(x))\equiv h_{1}(z_{1})-h_{3}(z_{3})
.

Next, by Claim
4.8
and
(\ast)_{1}
, we have

(\ast)_{3}

{\bf f}(h_{4}(x))\in\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h_{4})+({\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}))
.

So

(\ast)_{4}

h_{1}(z_{1})-h_{3}(z_{3})\in\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h_{4})+({\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}))
.

We combine
(\ast)_{4}
along with the definition of
h_{4}
to deduce that

(h_{1}(z_{1})-h_{3}(z_{3})-h_{4}(z)\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}),

for some
z\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)}
. Recall that
h_{4}(z)=h_{1}(z)-h_{3}(z)
. It yields that

(h_{1}(z_{1})-h_{3}(z_{3}))-(h_{1}(z)-h_{3}(z))\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}).

In other words,

h_{1}(z_{1}-z)-h_{3}(z_{3}-z)\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}).

Therefor, there is
y\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}
such that
h_{1}(z_{1}-z)-h_{3}(z_{3}-z)-y\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa})
. As
{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}^{\ast}_{1}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}^{\ast}_{3}\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
, we deduce that

h_{1}(z_{1}-z)-h_{3}(z_{3}-z)-y\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}^{\ast}_{1}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}^{\ast}_{3}).

Recall that
h_{1}(z_{1}-z)\in{\mathbb{N}}_{1},h_{3}(z_{3}-z)\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\ast}_{3}
and
y\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}
. We conclude from Claim

3.19
(5) that

\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha})\oplus\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{N}}^{\ast}_{1})\oplus\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{N}}^{\ast}_{3})\cong\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}^{\ast}_{1}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}^{\ast}_{3}).

Hence

h_{i}(z_{i}-z)\in h_{i}^{\prime\prime}\left(\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)})\right)\quad\mbox{ for }i=1,3,

i.e.,
z_{i}-z\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)}).

It then follows that

z_{1}-z_{3}=(z_{1}-z)-(z_{3}-z)\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)}).

So
z_{1}-z_{3}\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)})
,
which finishes the proof of the claim.
∎

Claim 4.10
.

There is
z\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)}
such that if
h:{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)}\to{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
is a bimodule homomorphism, then

{\bf f}(h(z))-h(z)\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\kappa}).

Proof.

Let
{\mathbb{N}}_{0}^{\ast}
,
\beta_{0}
and
h:{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)}\to{\mathbb{N}}_{0}^{\ast}
be as Claim
4.9
. In the light of Claim
4.8
, there is
z\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)}
which
satisfies the above
requirement for this
h
. We show that
z
is as required.
Suppose not and let
h_{0}
be a counterexample, i.e.,

{\bf f}(h_{0}(z))-h_{0}(z)\notin{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\kappa}).

Choose
\beta

such that

\beta_{0}<\beta\in\kappa\setminus S
and
\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h_{0})\subseteq{\mathbb{M}}_{\beta}
. Such a
\beta
exists as
\kappa=\mathop{\mathrm{cf}}(\kappa)\geq\kappa({\mathfrak{E}})
.
Let
h_{1}
be an isomorphism from

{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)}
onto some

{\mathbb{N}}^{\ast}_{1}
such that
{\mathbb{M}}_{\beta}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}^{\ast}_{1}\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\gamma}
for
some
\gamma\in(\beta,\kappa)\setminus S
. So

{\bf f}(h_{1}(z))-h_{1}(z)\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}).

Let
h_{2}:{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
be defined by

h_{2}(z)=h_{1}(z)-h_{0}(z).

Easily
h_{2}
is a bimodule homomorphism. Set
{\mathbb{N}}^{\ast}_{2}=:\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h_{2})
. By the assumptions on

{\mathbb{N}}^{\ast}_{1}
and
h_{1}
, we deduce that

{\mathbb{M}}_{\beta}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}^{\ast}_{1}={\mathbb{M}}_{\beta}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}^{\ast}_{2}\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}.

In the light of Claim
4.9
, we see that

{\mathbf{f}}(h_{2}(x))-h_{2}(z)\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}).

We apply this to conclude that

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\mathbf{f}}(h_{0}(z))&={\mathbf{f}}(h_{1}(z)-h_{2}(z))\\
&={\mathbf{f}}(h_{1}(z))-{\mathbf{f}}(h_{2}(z))\\
&\in h_{1}(z)-h_{2}(z)+{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa})\\
&=h_{0}(z)+{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}),\end{array}

i.e.,

{\bf f}(h_{0}(z))-h_{0}(z)\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\kappa}),

which contradicts our initial assumption on
h_{0}
. The claim follows.
∎

Claim 4.11
.

Let
z
be as Claim
4.10
. Then

z\in{\mathbb{L}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)}[{\mathcal{K}}]
.

Proof.

Let
h_{1},h_{2}:{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)}\to{\mathbb{N}}\in{\mathcal{K}}
be such that
h_{1}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)})-h_{2}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)})\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{N}})
and
{\mathbf{f}}(h_{i}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(*)}))=h_{i}(z)\mod{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}_{\lambda})
. By the definition of
{\mathbb{L}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)}[{\mathcal{K}}]
,
it is sufficient to show
h_{1}(z)-h_{2}(z)\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{N}})
.
To this end, choose
\gamma\in\kappa\setminus S
large enough and an embedding
h

of
{\mathbb{N}}
into
{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
such that

{\mathbb{M}}_{\gamma}+\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h)={\mathbb{M}}_{\gamma}\oplus\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h)\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
. Let
i=1,2
and
note that
h\circ h_{i}
is a bimodule homomorphism from

{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(*)}

into
{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
.
Thanks to Claim
4.10
we observe that

{\mathbf{f}}(h(h_{i}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(*)})))-{\mathbf{f}}(h(h_{i}(z)))\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}).

So by subtracting the above relations for
i=1,2
we have

{\mathbf{f}}(h(h_{1}-h_{2}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)})))-{\mathbf{f}}(h(h_{1}-h_{2}(z)))\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}).

Also,
h_{1}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(*)})=h_{2}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(*)})\mod\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{N}})
. It follows that
h(h_{1}-h_{2}(z))\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa})
. We conclude from
{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}+\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h)={\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}\oplus\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h)
that

h(h_{1}-h_{2}(z))\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}(\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h)).

Since
h

is an embedding,
(h_{1}-h_{2})(z)\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{N}}).

So

h_{1}(z)-h_{2}(z)=(h_{1}-h_{2})(z)\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{N}})
. By definition,
z\in{\mathbb{L}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)}[{\mathcal{K}}]
as required.
∎

In sum, Lemma
4.7
follows.
∎

Lemma 4.12
.

Let
\bar{{\mathbb{M}}}
be a semi-nice construction for
(\lambda,{\mathfrak{m}},S,\kappa)
and
{\mathbf{f}}
be an
{\bf R}
-endomorphism of
{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
. Then for some
\alpha<\kappa,~n(*)<\omega
and
z\in{\mathbb{L}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(*)}
the statement

({\mathord{\mathrm{Pr}}}1)^{n(*)}_{\alpha,z}[{\mathbf{f}},\mathfrak{e}]
holds.

Proof.

In the light of Lemma
4.2
, we can find
\alpha\in\kappa\setminus S
and
n(*)<\omega
such that
({\mathord{\mathrm{Pr}}})^{n(*)}_{\alpha}[{\mathbf{f}},\mathfrak{e}]

holds. According to Lemma
4.7
, there exists
z\in{\mathbb{L}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(*)}
such that

({\mathord{\mathrm{Pr}}}1)^{n(*)}_{\alpha,z}[{\mathbf{f}},\mathfrak{e}]
holds, as claimed.
∎

Remark 4.13
.

The property
({\mathord{\mathrm{Pr}}}1)^{n(\ast)}_{\alpha,z}[{\mathbf{f}},{\mathfrak{e}}]

is almost what is required, only the “error term”
{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}

is too large.
However, before we do this, we note that for the solution of
Kaplansky test problems, as done later in Section
6
, this improvement is immaterial as we just
divide by a stronger ideal, i.e., we allow to divide by a submodule of bigger
cardinality.

Definition 4.14
.

Assume
\bar{\mathbb{M}}=\langle{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}:\alpha\leq\kappa\rangle

is a weakly semi-nice construction for
(\lambda,{\mathfrak{m}},S,\kappa)
,
{\mathbf{f}}
is an
{\bf R}
-endomorphism of
{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
,
{\mathfrak{e}}\in{\mathfrak{E}}^{\mathfrak{m}}
and
n(\ast)<\omega
. Let also

\bar{\mathbb{G}}:=\langle{\mathbb{G}}_{n}:n\geq n(*)\rangle
be a sequence of additive subgroups of
\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa})
.

(1)

We say
\bar{\mathbb{G}}
is
\bar{\varphi}
-appropriate for

{\mathbb{M}}
if
{\mathbb{G}}_{n}\subseteq\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa})
for
all
n\geq n(*).

(2)

We say
\bar{\mathbb{G}}
is compact for
(\bar{\varphi}^{\mathfrak{e}},n(*))
in
{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
, if it is
\bar{\varphi}
-appropriate and for any
z^{*}_{\ell}\in{\mathbb{G}}_{\ell}
with
\ell\geq n(\ast)
, there is
z^{*}\in{\mathbb{G}}_{n(*)}
such that

z^{*}-\sum\limits_{\ell=n(*)}^{n}z^{*}_{\ell}\in\varphi_{n+1}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}).

(3)

We say

{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
is

(\mathcal{K},\bar{\varphi})
-finitary in
{\mathbb{M}}
, if
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\subseteq\sum\limits_{\ell<n}{\mathbb{K}}_{\ell}+\varphi_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa})
for some finite
n<\omega

and
{\mathbb{K}}_{\ell}\in c\ell_{\text{is}}(\mathcal{K})
such that
\sum\limits_{\ell<n}{\mathbb{K}}_{\ell}\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}
for
\alpha

large enough in
\kappa\setminus S
.

(4)

We say
\bar{\mathbb{G}}
is
(\mathcal{K},\bar{\varphi})
-finitary in
{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
if
~{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
is

(\mathcal{K},\bar{\varphi})
-finitary in
{\mathbb{M}}
for some
m\geq n(\ast)
.

(5)

We say
\bar{\mathbb{G}}
is non-trivial if
\mathbb{G}_{m}\nsubseteq\varphi_{\omega}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa})
for all
m\geq n(\ast)
.

We need to present another definition:

Definition 4.15
.

Assume
\bar{\mathbb{M}}=\langle{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}:\alpha\leq\kappa\rangle

is a weakly semi-nice construction for
(\lambda,{\mathfrak{m}},S,\kappa)
. Let
{\mathbf{f}}
be any
{\bf R}
-endomorphism of
{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
and
{\mathfrak{e}}\in{\mathfrak{E}}^{\mathfrak{m}}
. We replace weakly by strongly
from Definition

3.32
provided the following properties denoted by
(G)^{+}
, are valid:

(1)

there is some
\alpha<\kappa
such that
|{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}||<\lambda
;

(2)

there is some
n(*)<\omega
and

z\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)}
such that for every bimodule homomorphism
h:{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(*)}\rightarrow{\mathbb{M}}
we have

{\mathbf{f}}h(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(*)})-h(z)\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}+\varphi_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}).

Definition 4.16
.

In the previous definition, we replace strongly by
{\rm strong}^{+}
if

{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}
is replaced by

{\mathbb{M}}_{*}\oplus{\mathbb{K}}\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}

for some
{\mathbb{K}}\in c\ell_{\text{is}}(\mathcal{K}).

Lemma 4.17
.

Assume
\kappa=\lambda
and

\bar{\mathbb{M}}=\langle{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}:\alpha\leq\kappa\rangle
is
a semi-nice construction for

(\lambda,{{\mathfrak{m}}},S,\kappa)
such that
\alpha<\kappa,~||{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}||<\lambda
.
Suppose
{\mathfrak{e}}\in{{\mathfrak{E}}}
and
{\mathbf{f}}
is an
{\bf R}
-endomorphism of

{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
, for some

n(\ast)<\omega
,
\alpha(\ast)\in\kappa\setminus S
and
z\in{\mathbb{L}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)}[{\mathcal{K}}]
such that the property

({\mathord{\mathrm{Pr}}}1)^{n(\ast)}_{\alpha(\ast),z}[{\mathbf{f}},{\mathfrak{e}}]
holds.
13
13

13

Such a thing exists by Lemma
4.12
.

Then there exists a decreasing sequence
\bar{\mathbb{G}}^{\ast}=\langle{\mathbb{G}}^{\ast}_{n}:n\geq n(\ast)\rangle
of additive subgroups of
\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa})
, satisfying
{\mathbb{G}}^{\ast}_{n}\subseteq\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa})
and equip with the following properties:

(1)

for every
n\geq n(\ast)
and every bimodule-homomorphism

h:{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
,

(a)

{\mathbf{f}}(h(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}))-h(z_{n})\in{\mathbb{G}}^{\ast}_{n}
where

z_{n}:=g^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast),n}(z)
,

(b)

{\mathbb{G}}_{n}\subseteq{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha(\ast)}+\varphi_{\omega}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa})
.

(2)

\bar{\mathbb{G}}^{\ast}
is compact for

\bar{\varphi}^{\mathfrak{e}}
.

Proof.

For every
n\geq n(\ast)
we define
{\mathbb{G}}^{\ast}_{n}
by

{\mathbb{G}}^{\ast}_{n}:=\big\{{\mathbf{f}}(h(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}))-h(z_{n}):h:{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}\to{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}\mbox{
is a bimodule homomorphism}\big\}.

It is easily seen that

{\mathbb{G}}^{\ast}_{n}\subseteq{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha(\ast)}+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa})
. Indeed let
h:{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}\to{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
be
a bimodule homomorphism. Then
h^{\prime}=h\circ g^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast),n}:{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)}\to{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
is a bimodule
homomorphism. In view of
({\mathord{\mathrm{Pr}}}1)^{n(\ast)}_{\alpha(\ast),z}[{\mathbf{f}},{\mathfrak{e}}]
we observe that

{\mathbf{f}}(h(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}))-h(z_{n})={\mathbf{f}}(h^{\prime}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)}))-h^{\prime}(z)\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha(\ast)}+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}).

Also as
x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n},z\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n})
,
we have
{\mathbf{f}}(h(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}))-h(z_{n})\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa})
. Clearly,

{\mathbb{G}}^{\ast}_{n}
is an additive
subgroup of
\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa})
and the sequence
\bar{\mathbb{G}}
is decreasing.

Let us prove clause (2). Suppose
z^{\ast}_{\ell}\in{\mathbb{G}}^{\ast}_{\ell}
for

n(\ast)\leq\ell<\omega
, so for some bimodule homomorphism

h_{\ell}:{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
we have

z^{\ast}_{\ell}={\mathbf{f}}(h_{\ell}(x_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}))-h_{\ell}(z_{\ell}).

Let
\alpha(0)
with
\alpha(\ast)<\alpha(0)<\kappa
be such that
\alpha(0)\notin S
,
and for
n(\ast)\leq\ell,

\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h_{\ell})\subseteq{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha(0)}
and

{\mathbf{f}}(h_{\ell}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell}))\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha(0)}
. Note that such
\alpha(0)
necessarily exists as
\kappa=\mathop{\mathrm{cf}}(\kappa)\geq\kappa({\mathfrak{E}})+\aleph_{0}
. We need the following claim:

Claim 4.18
.

For each
n\geq n(\ast)
and
\beta>\alpha(1)
with
\beta\in\kappa\setminus S
, there are
\gamma
satisfying

\beta<\gamma\in\kappa\setminus S
, some embedding
h_{\beta,n}:{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{M}}_{\gamma}
and some
{\mathbb{K}}_{\beta,n}\in c\ell^{\aleph_{0}}_{is}({\mathcal{K}})
such that the following two items hold:

(1)

{\mathbb{M}}_{\beta}\oplus\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h_{\beta,n})\oplus{\mathbb{K}}_{\beta,n}\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\gamma},

(2)

{\mathbf{f}}(h_{\beta,n}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}))\in\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h_{\beta,n})\oplus{\mathbb{K}}_{\beta,n}
.

In particular,

{\mathbf{f}}(h_{\beta,n}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}))-h_{\beta,n}(z_{n})\in\varphi_{\omega}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa})
.

Proof.

Fix
n
and
\beta
as in the claim. For every
\gamma

satisfying
\beta<\gamma\in\kappa\setminus S
, let
h_{\gamma}:{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{M}}_{\gamma+1}
and
{\mathbb{K}}^{0}_{\gamma}\in c\ell_{is}({\mathcal{K}})
be such that
h_{\gamma}
is a bimodule embedding and

{\mathbf{f}}(h_{\gamma}(x_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}))\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\gamma}\oplus\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h_{\gamma})\oplus{\mathbb{K}}^{0}_{\gamma}\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}.

Let
\epsilon_{\gamma}>\gamma
be in
\kappa\setminus S
such that
{\mathbf{f}}

maps
{\mathbb{M}}_{\epsilon_{\gamma}}
into
{\mathbb{M}}_{\epsilon_{\gamma}}

and

{\mathbb{M}}_{\gamma}\oplus\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h_{\gamma})\oplus{\mathbb{K}}^{0}_{\gamma}\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\epsilon_{\gamma}}.

Let
{\mathbf{f}}(h_{\gamma}(x_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}))=z^{1}_{\gamma}+z^{2}_{\gamma}+z^{3}_{\gamma}
, where
z^{1}_{\gamma}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\gamma}
,
z^{2}_{\gamma}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h_{\gamma})
and

z^{3}_{\gamma}\in{\mathbb{K}}^{0}_{\gamma}
. By
Fodor’s lemma for some
z
and for a stationary set
T\subseteq\kappa\setminus S
we have

•

\mathop{\mathrm{min}}(T)>\beta,

•

z^{1}_{\gamma}=z
, for all
\gamma\in T.

Let
\gamma(1)
,
\gamma(2)\in T
be such that
\epsilon_{\gamma(1)}<\gamma(2)
. Now, the following

(i)

\gamma:=\epsilon_{\gamma(2)},

(ii)

h_{\beta,n}:=h_{\gamma(2)}-h_{\gamma(1)}
and

(iii)

{\mathbb{K}}_{\beta,n}:={\mathbb{K}}^{0}_{\gamma(1)}\oplus{\mathbb{K}}^{0}_{\gamma(2)}\oplus\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h_{\gamma(1)})

are as required.

The particular case follows by the
choose of

\alpha(\ast)
.
∎

Let us complete the proof of Lemma
4.17
. To this end, we look at the following club of
\kappa
:

A:=\{\beta<\kappa:\beta>\alpha(0)\text{~and~}{\mathbf{f}}^{\prime\prime}({\mathbb{M}}_{\beta})\subseteq{\mathbb{M}}_{\beta}\}.

Let
n\geq n(\ast)
and
\beta\in A\cap(\kappa\setminus S).
According to Claim
4.18
, we can find
some
\gamma_{\beta}>\beta
a bimodule embedding

h_{\beta,n}:{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{M}}_{\gamma_{\beta}}
, and
{\mathbb{K}}_{\beta,n}\in c\ell^{\aleph_{0}}_{is}({\mathcal{K}})
such that

•

{\mathbb{M}}_{\beta}\oplus\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h_{\beta,n})\oplus{\mathbb{K}}_{\beta,n}\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\gamma_{\beta}},

•

{\mathbf{f}}(h_{\beta,n}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}))\in\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}(h_{\beta,n})\oplus{\mathbb{K}}_{\beta,n}
.

Set
{\mathcal{U}}=:\{\ell:n(\ast)\leq\ell<\omega\}
and
{\mathfrak{e}}^{\ast}={\mathfrak{e}}\restriction{\mathcal{U}}
, so
{\mathfrak{e}}^{*}\in{{\mathfrak{E}}}^{{\mathfrak{m}}}
as well. For
\ell\in{\mathcal{U}}
and
\beta\in A\cap(\kappa\setminus S),
set

h^{\prime}_{\beta,\ell}=h_{\beta,\ell}+h_{\ell}.

We are going to use the property presented in part
(G)_{2}
of Definition
3.33
(3). In this regard, we can find an embedding
{\bf h}:{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}^{\ast}}\to{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}

and an increasing sequence
\langle\epsilon_{n}:n<\omega\rangle
of elements of
A\cap(\kappa\setminus S)

such that
\epsilon=\mathop{\mathrm{sup}}\limits_{n<\omega}\epsilon_{n}\in A\cap(\kappa\setminus S)
and
for each
n\in{\mathcal{U}},h^{\prime}_{\epsilon_{n},n}={\bf h}\circ h_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}^{\ast}}
.
Then

{\mathbf{f}}(h^{\prime}_{\epsilon_{\ell},\ell}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell}))-h^{\prime}_{\epsilon_{\ell},\ell}(z_{\ell})={\mathbf{f}}(h_{\epsilon_{\ell},\ell}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell}))-h_{\epsilon_{\ell},\ell}(z_{\ell})-z^{\ast}_{\ell}\in z^{\ast}_{\ell}+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}).

Let
x:={\bf f}_{n(\ast)}^{\mathfrak{e}}(x_{1}^{\mathfrak{e}^{\ast}})
and
z^{\prime}:={\bf f}_{n(\ast)}^{\mathfrak{e}}(z)
where
{\bf f}_{n(\ast)}^{\mathfrak{e}}

were defined in Lemma
3.28
.
It then follows that

•

x-\sum\limits_{\ell=n(\ast)}^{\ell=n-1}h_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}}(x_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}})\in\varphi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}})
, and

•

z^{\prime}-\sum\limits_{\ell=n(\ast)}^{\ell=n-1}h_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}}(z_{\ell})\in\varphi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}}).

We deduce from these memberships that

{\bf h}(x)-\sum\limits_{\ell=n(\ast)}^{\ell=n-1}{\bf h}(h_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}}(x_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}}))\in\varphi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa})

and

{\bf h}(z^{\prime})-\sum\limits_{\ell=n(\ast)}^{\ell=n-1}{\bf h}(h_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}}(z_{\ell}))\in\varphi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}).

As
\bf f
is an endomorphism, we have

{\bf f}({\bf h}(x))-\sum\limits_{\ell=n(\ast)}^{\ell=n-1}{\bf f}({\bf h}(h_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}}(x_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}})))\in\varphi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}).

We plug this in the previous formula and deduce that

{\bf f}({\bf h}(x))-{\bf h}(z^{\prime})-\sum\limits_{\ell=n(\ast)}^{\ell=n-1}({\bf f}({\bf h}(h_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}}(x_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}})))-{\bf h}(h_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}}(z_{\ell})))\in\varphi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}).

Note that for some
{\mathbb{K}}\in c\ell(\mathcal{K}),{\mathbb{M}}_{\epsilon}={\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha(0)}\oplus{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathfrak{e}}\oplus{\mathbb{K}}
. Let

\pi:{\mathbb{M}}_{\epsilon}\to{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha(0)}

be the projection map and set

z^{\ast}:=\pi({\bf f}({\bf h}(x)))-\pi({\bf h}(z^{\prime})).

We apply
h^{\prime}_{\epsilon_{\ell},\ell}={\bf h}\circ h_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}^{\ast}}
along with the previous observation to see:

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\bf f}({\bf h}(h_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}}(x_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}})))-{\bf h}(h_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}}(z_{\ell}))&={\mathbf{f}}(h^{\prime}_{\epsilon_{\ell},\ell}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell}))-h^{\prime}_{\epsilon_{\ell},\ell}(z_{\ell})\\
&={\mathbf{f}}(h_{\epsilon_{\ell},\ell}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell}))-h_{\epsilon_{\ell},\ell}(z_{\ell})-z^{\ast}_{\ell}.\end{array}

This yields that

\begin{array}[]{ll}\pi({\bf f}({\bf h}(h_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}}(x_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}}))))-\pi({\bf h}(h_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}}(z_{\ell})))&=\pi({\mathbf{f}}(h_{\epsilon_{\ell},\ell}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell})))-\pi(h_{\epsilon_{\ell},\ell}(z_{\ell}))-\pi(z^{\ast}_{\ell})\\
&=z^{\ast}_{\ell}.\end{array}

Therefore,

\begin{array}[]{ll}z^{\ast}-\sum\limits^{n-1}_{\ell=n(\ast)}z^{*}_{\ell}&=\pi({\bf f}({\bf h}(x)))-\pi({\bf h}(z^{\prime}))-\sum\limits_{\ell=n(\ast)}^{\ell=n-1}\pi({\bf f}({\bf h}(h_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}}(x_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}}))))-\pi({\bf h}(h_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}}(z_{\ell})))\\
&\in\varphi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}).\end{array}

So,
z^{\ast}
is as required.
∎

Let
z\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)}
. Following the above lemma, let us define,

{{\mathbb{G}}}^{\mathfrak{m}}_{n,z}[\bar{\mathbb{M}}]
as

\langle{\mathbb{G}}_{n}:n\geq n(*)\rangle
where

{\mathbb{G}}_{n}:=\{{\mathbf{f}}(h(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}))-h(g^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(*),n}(z)):h\in\textmd{Hom}({\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n},{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa})\}.

We now show that under extra assumptions we can get a better
{\mathbb{G}}_{n}
’s.

Lemma 4.19
.

Let
\bar{\mathbb{M}}=\langle{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}:\alpha\leq\kappa\rangle

be a weakly semi-nice construction for
(\lambda,{\mathfrak{m}},S,\kappa)
,

{\mathbf{f}}
be an
{\bf R}
-endomorphism,
{\mathfrak{e}}\in{\mathfrak{E}}^{\mathfrak{m}}
and
n(\ast)<\omega
.

(1)

If
{\mathbb{K}}=\mathop{{\bigoplus}}\limits_{t\in I}{\mathbb{K}}_{t}

(as
{\bf R}
-modules),
\bar{\mathbb{G}}={{\mathbb{G}}}^{\mathfrak{m}}_{n,z}[\bar{\mathbb{M}}]

is decreasing and
compact for

(\bar{\varphi}^{\mathfrak{e}},n(\ast))
in
{\mathbb{K}}
over

{\mathbb{K}}_{0}
, then for
some finite
J\subseteq I
and
m<\omega
:

{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\subseteq\mathop{{\bigoplus}}\limits_{t\in J}{\mathbb{K}}_{t}+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{K}}).

(2)

Suppose
{\mathbb{K}}=\sum\limits_{t\in I}{\mathbb{K}}_{t}
(as
{\bf R}
–modules) and

if
J_{1}
,
J_{2}
are finite subsets of
I
,
n<\omega
,

y_{\ell}\in\sum\limits_{t\in J_{\ell}}{\mathbb{K}}_{t}
for

\ell=1,2
,

(\ast):

and if
y_{1}-y_{2}\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{K}})
, then for some

y_{3}\in\sum\limits_{t}\{{\mathbb{K}}_{t}:t\in J_{1}\cap J_{2}\}

we have

y_{1}-y_{3}\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{K}})
and

y_{3}-y_{2}\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{K}})
.

Then for
some finite
J\subseteq I
and
m<\omega
:

{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\subseteq\mathop{{\bigoplus}}\limits_{t\in J}{\mathbb{K}}_{t}+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{K}}).

(3)

Suppose
\bar{\mathbb{G}}
is compact for

(\bar{\varphi}^{\mathfrak{e}},n(\ast))
in

{\mathbb{K}}
(as
{\bf R}
-modules),
h:{\mathbb{K}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{K}}^{\prime}
is an
{\bf R}
-homomorphism and for

h(x)\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell}({\mathbb{K}}^{\prime})\setminus\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell+1}({\mathbb{K}}^{\prime})

there exists
y\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell}({\mathbb{K}})\setminus\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell+1}({\mathbb{K}})
such that

h(y)=h(x)
.
Then
{h^{\prime\prime}}(\bar{\mathbb{G}})=\langle{h^{\prime\prime}}({\mathbb{G}}_{n}):n\geq n(\ast)\rangle
is
compact for
(\bar{\varphi}^{\mathfrak{e}},n(\ast))
in
{\mathbb{K}}^{\prime}
.

(4)

If
{\mathbb{G}}\subseteq{\mathbb{K}}
,
{\mathbb{K}}=\mathop{{\bigoplus}}\limits^{n}_{t=1}{\mathbb{K}}_{t}
and
the projection of
{\mathbb{G}}
to each
{\mathbb{K}}^{t}
is

({\mathcal{K}},\bar{\varphi})
-finitary,
then
{\mathbb{G}}
in
(\mathcal{K},\bar{\varphi})
-finitary.

(5)

If
{\mathbb{K}}_{0}\subseteq{\mathbb{K}}_{1}\leq^{ads}_{{\mathcal{K}},\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{K}}_{2}
and
\bar{\mathbb{G}}
is
(\bar{\varphi}^{\mathfrak{e}},n(*))
-compact in
{\mathbb{K}}_{2}
over
{\mathbb{K}}_{0}
,
then
\langle{\mathbb{G}}_{n}\cap{\mathbb{K}}_{1}:n\geq n(*)\rangle
is

(\bar{\varphi}^{\mathfrak{e}},n(*))
-compact in
{\mathbb{K}}_{1}
over
{\mathbb{K}}_{0}
.

Proof.

(1) Let us first suppose that the index set
I
is countable. Suppose by contradiction that for all finite
J\subseteq I

and
m<\omega
,

{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\nsubseteq\mathop{{\bigoplus}}\limits_{t\in J}{\mathbb{K}}_{t}+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{K}}).

We argue by induction on
\ell\geq n(\ast)
to find
z_{\ell}
,

J_{\ell}
and
n_{\ell}
such that the following properties hold:

i)

J_{\ell}
is a finite subset of
I
,

ii)

J_{\ell}\subseteq J_{\ell+1}
,

iii)

I=\bigcup\limits_{\ell<\omega}J_{\ell},

iv)

z_{\ell}\in{\mathbb{G}}_{\ell}\setminus\left(\mathop{{\bigoplus}}\limits_{t\in J_{\ell}}K_{t}+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{K}})\right)
,

v)

z_{\ell}\notin\mathop{{\bigoplus}}\limits_{t\in J_{\ell}}K_{t}+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n_{\ell+1}}({\mathbb{K}})
,

vi)

z_{\ell}\in\mathop{{\bigoplus}}\limits_{t\in J_{\ell+1}}{\mathbb{K}}_{t}
.

Let
\mathcal{U}
be an infinite subset of
\omega
such that
0\in\mathcal{U}
and

\ell<m\mbox{ and }m\in\mathcal{U}\implies n_{\ell+1}<m.

Define
\langle z^{*}_{\ell}:\ell<\omega\rangle
by
z^{*}_{\ell}=z_{\ell}
if
\ell\in\mathcal{U}
and
z^{*}_{\ell}=0
otherwise.
By compactness, we can find
z^{\ast}\in{\mathbb{G}}_{n(\ast)}
such that for each

\ell\geq n(\ast)
,

z^{\ast}-\sum\limits_{i=n(\ast)}^{\ell}z^{*}_{i}\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell+1}({\mathbb{K}}).

Let
J\subseteq I
be a finite set such that
z^{\ast}\in\bigoplus\limits_{t\in J}{\mathbb{K}}_{t}
. Pick
m\in\mathcal{U}

such that
J\subseteq J_{m}
and set
n
be the least element of
\mathcal{U}
above
m
. Then
z_{m}=z^{*}_{m}
and

z^{\ast}-\sum\limits_{i=n(\ast)}^{m}z^{*}_{i}=z^{\ast}-\sum\limits_{i=n(\ast)}^{n-1}z^{*}_{i}\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{K}}).

An easy contradiction.

Let us now show that we can get the result for arbitrary
I
. Thus suppose
I
is uncountable and suppose that the conclusion of the lemma fails
for it. Construct
z_{\ell},J_{\ell},n_{\ell}
as before and set
\bar{I}=\bigcup\limits_{\ell=n(\ast)}^{\omega}J_{\ell}.
Then
\bar{I}
is countable. Set

\bar{\mathbb{K}}=\bigoplus\limits_{t\in\bar{I}}{\mathbb{K}}_{t}

and
\bar{\mathbb{G}}_{n}={\mathbb{G}}_{n}\cap\bar{\mathbb{K}}
. It then follows that the conclusion fails for this case, contradicting the above argument.

(2). This is similar to
(1)
.

(3). Suppose
h(x_{\ell})\in h^{\prime\prime}({\mathbb{G}}_{\ell})
, for
\ell\geq n(\ast)
. By our assumption, we can find a sequence
\langle y_{\ell}:\ell\geq n(\ast)\rangle
such that

•

y_{\ell}\in{\mathbb{G}}_{\ell}
,

•

h(y_{\ell})=h(x_{\ell})
,

•

h(x_{\ell})\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell}({\mathbb{K}}^{\prime})\setminus\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell+1}({\mathbb{K}}^{\prime})\implies y_{\ell}\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell}({\mathbb{K}})\setminus\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell+1}({\mathbb{K}})
.

Let
z\in{\mathbb{G}}_{n(\ast)}
be such that for each
n\geq n(\ast),z-\sum\limits_{\ell=n(\ast)}^{n}y_{\ell}\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n+1}({\mathbb{K}})
. This implies that

h(z)-\sum\limits_{\ell=n(\ast)}^{n}h(y_{\ell})\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n+1}({\mathbb{K}}).

Thus
h(z)\in h^{\prime\prime}({\mathbb{G}}_{n(\ast)})
is as required.

(4). For
t=1,\cdots,t
let
\pi_{t}:{\mathbb{K}}\to{\mathbb{K}}_{t}
be the projection map to
{\mathbb{K}}_{t}
. We have

\pi_{t}^{\prime\prime}({\mathbb{G}})\subseteq\sum\limits_{i<m_{t}}{\mathbb{K}}_{i}^{t}+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{K}}),

where each
{\mathbb{K}}_{i}^{t}
is in
c\ell_{\text{is}}({\mathbb{K}})
. Then

{\mathbb{G}}=\sum\limits_{t=1}^{n}\pi_{t}^{\prime\prime}({\mathbb{G}})\subseteq\sum\limits_{t=1}^{n}\left(\sum\limits_{i<m_{t}}{\mathbb{K}}_{i}^{t}+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{K}})\right)\subseteq\sum\limits_{t=1}^{n}\sum\limits_{i<m_{t}}{\mathbb{K}}_{i}^{t}+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{K}}).

We are done.

(5). For each
\ell\geq n(\ast)
,
we peak

z_{\ell}\in{\mathbb{G}}_{\ell}\cap{\mathbb{K}}_{1}
. Due to our assumption, there exists
z^{\ast}\in{\mathbb{K}}_{2}
such that
z^{\ast}-\sum\limits_{\ell=n(\ast)}^{n}z_{\ell}\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n+1}({\mathbb{K}}_{2})
for all
n\geq n(\ast)
. Consequently,

{\mathbb{K}}_{2}\models~\exists z^{\ast}\bigwedge\limits_{n=n(\ast)}^{\omega}\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n+1}(z^{\ast}-\sum\limits_{\ell=n(\ast)}^{n}z_{\ell}).

As
{\mathbb{K}}_{1}\leq^{ads}_{{\mathcal{K}},\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{K}}_{2}
and in the light of Lemma
3.18
(1) we have

{\mathbb{K}}_{1}\models~\exists z^{\ast}\bigwedge\limits_{n=n(\ast)}^{\omega}\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n+1}(z^{\ast}-\sum\limits_{\ell=n(\ast)}^{n}z_{\ell}).

Let
z^{\ast}\in{\mathbb{K}}_{1}
be witness this. Then,
z^{\ast}-\sum\limits_{\ell=n(\ast)}^{n}z_{\ell}\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n+1}({\mathbb{K}}_{1})

for all
n\geq n(\ast),
as required.
∎

Remark 4.20
.

i)

In Lemma
4.19
(3), we can weaken the assumption on
h
to:
for some
\eta\in{}^{\omega}\omega
diverging to infinity
if
\ell\geq n(\ast)
and
h(x)\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell}({\mathbb{K}}^{\prime})\setminus\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell+1}({\mathbb{K}}^{\prime})
, then

h(x)=h(y)
for some
y\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)}({\mathbb{K}})\setminus\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\eta(\ell)}({\mathbb{K}}).

ii)

Note that if
h
is a projection, then it satisfies in the presented condition from the first item.

Lemma 4.21
.

Let
{\bf R}
,
{\bf S}
and every
{\mathbb{N}}\in{\mathcal{K}}

be of cardinality
<2^{\aleph_{0}}
and let

{\mathfrak{e}}\in{{\mathfrak{E}}}
. Then there is no non-trivial compact

\bar{\mathbb{G}}
for
\bar{\varphi}^{\mathfrak{e}}

in any
{\mathcal{K}}
-bimodule
{\mathbb{M}}
.

Proof.

Let

\langle{\mathbb{G}}_{n}:n\geq n_{0}\rangle
be
(\bar{\varphi}^{\mathfrak{e}},n_{0})
-compact
in

{\mathbb{M}}
. According to Definition
4.14
(4) we need to show that

{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\subseteq\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}})

for some
m
.
Suppose on the contradiction that
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\nsubseteq\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}})
for all
m\geq n_{0}
. Hence,
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\nsubseteq\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell_{m}}({\mathbb{M}})

for some
\ell_{m}>n_{0}
. Pick
z_{m}\in{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\setminus\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ell_{m}}({\mathbb{M}})
, for
m\geq n_{0}
.
For any infinite
\mathcal{U}\subseteq\omega\setminus n_{0}
, find
z_{\mathcal{U}}\in{\mathbb{G}}_{n_{0}}
such that

~z_{\mathcal{U}}-\sum\{z_{m}:m\in\mathcal{U}\cap[n_{0},n]\}\in\varphi_{n+1}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}).

for all
n\in\mathcal{U}
.
Let
\mathcal{U}_{1}
and
\mathcal{U}_{2}
be two subsets of
\omega
with finite intersection property. Then
z_{\mathcal{U}_{1}}\neq z_{\mathcal{U}_{2}}.
It follows that

2^{\aleph_{0}}\leq||{\mathbb{G}}_{n_{0}}||\leq||{\mathbb{M}}||<2^{\aleph_{0}},

which is impossible.
∎

Lemma 4.22
.

Under the same assumptions as in Lemma
4.17
,
{{\mathbb{G}}}^{\mathfrak{m}}_{n,z}[\bar{\mathbb{M}}]=\langle{\mathbb{G}}_{n}:n\geq n(\ast)\rangle
is
(\mathcal{K},\bar{\varphi}^{\mathfrak{e}})
-finitary in

{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
. Furthermore, if for
{\mathbb{N}}\in\mathcal{K},
there is no non-trivial
\bar{\mathbb{L}}=\langle\mathbb{L}_{n}:n\geq n(\ast)\rangle
compact for
(\bar{\varphi}^{\mathfrak{e}},n(\ast))

in
{\mathbb{M}}_{\ast}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}
, then, by increasing
n(\ast)
, we can take
{{\mathbb{G}}}^{\mathfrak{m}}_{n,z}[\bar{\mathbb{M}}]=\bar{0},
i.e.,
{\mathbb{G}}_{n}=0
for all
n\geq n(\ast)
.

Proof.

Pick
\alpha(\ast)\in\kappa\setminus S
such that
z\in{\mathbb{L}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n(\ast)}[{\mathcal{K}}]\subseteq{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha(\ast)}
.
We use the assumption
{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha(\ast)}\in c\ell(\mathcal{K})
along with Lemma
4.19
(1) to
find
m<\omega
and a finite subset
\{{\mathbb{K}}_{0},\cdots,{\mathbb{K}}_{n-1}\}
of
c\ell_{\text{is}}(\mathcal{K})
such that

{\mathbb{K}}_{0},\cdots,{\mathbb{K}}_{n-1}
are direct summands of
{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha(\ast)}
and

{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\subseteq\sum\limits_{\ell<n}{\mathbb{K}}_{\ell}+\varphi_{\omega}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}).

Recall from Lemma
3.34
(1) that
\sum\limits_{\ell<n}{\mathbb{K}}_{\ell}\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha(\ast)}\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}
, where
\alpha>\alpha(\ast)
. Thus

{{\mathbb{G}}}^{\mathfrak{m}}_{n,z}[\bar{\mathbb{M}}]=\langle{\mathbb{G}}_{n}:n\geq n(\ast)\rangle
is
(\mathcal{K},\bar{\varphi}^{\mathfrak{e}})
-finitary in

{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
.

Now suppose that for each
{\mathbb{N}}\in\mathcal{K},
there is no non-trivial
\bar{\mathbb{L}}=\langle\mathbb{L}_{n}:n\geq n(\ast)\rangle
compact for
(\bar{\varphi}^{\mathfrak{e}},n(\ast))

in
{\mathbb{M}}_{\ast}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}
. Suppose by contradiction that
{{\mathbb{G}}}^{\mathfrak{m}}_{n,z}[\bar{\mathbb{M}}]\neq\bar{0}.

Let
\alpha(\ast)
,
\{{\mathbb{K}}_{0},\cdots,{\mathbb{K}}_{n-1}\}\subset c\ell_{\text{is}}(\mathcal{K})

and
m
be as above. By increasing
n(\ast)
we may assume that
m=n(\ast)
.
Then

\bigoplus_{\ell<n}{\mathbb{K}}_{\ell}
is a direct summand of
{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha(\ast)}
. Suppose
{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha(\ast)}=\bigoplus_{\ell<n}{\mathbb{K}}_{\ell}\oplus{\mathbb{M}}
and we look at the natural projection map
\pi:{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha(\ast)}\to{\mathbb{M}}
. In view of Lemma
4.19
(3) (and Remark
4.20
) we observe that

\pi^{\prime\prime}({{\mathbb{G}}}^{\mathfrak{m}}_{n,z}[\bar{\mathbb{M}}])
is non-trivial and compact for
(\bar{\varphi}^{\mathfrak{e}},n(\ast))

in
{\mathbb{M}}_{\ast}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}
,
which is a contradiction.
∎

Remark 4.23
.

(1)

Suppose for every
{\mathcal{K}}
-bimodule

{\mathbb{M}}
and
{\mathfrak{e}}\in{{\mathfrak{E}}}
and
{\mathbb{G}}_{n}\subseteq{\mathbb{M}}
for

n\geq n_{0}
, if

\langle{\mathbb{G}}_{n}:n\geq n_{0}\rangle
is
(\bar{\varphi}^{\mathfrak{e}},n_{0})
-compact
in

{\mathbb{M}}
, then for
some
m
,
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\subseteq\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}})
. Recall that Lemma
4.21
presents a situation for which this property holds.
In view of Lemma

4.17
we
can choose
{\mathbb{G}}_{n(\ast)}=0
. In particular, the
“error term” disappears, i.e., for
every endomorphism
{\mathbf{f}}

of
{\mathbb{M}}_{\lambda}
as an
{\bf R}
–module, for some
m
we
have
{\mathbf{f}}\restriction\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{m}({\mathbb{M}}_{\lambda})/\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}_{\lambda})
is equal to

{\bf h}^{{\mathfrak{e}},m}_{{\mathbb{M}}_{\lambda},z}
(for its definition, see Definition
5.12
below).

(2)

If
{\bf R}
,
{\bf S}
have cardinality
<2^{\aleph_{0}}
, we have interesting
such
{\mathcal{K}}
’s, for example
{\mathcal{K}}
the family of finitely generated
finitely presented bimodules.

5.
More specific rings and families
{{\mathfrak{E}}}

In this section we introduce some special rings that play an important role in our solution of Kaplansky test problems. We also
specify some specific elements of
{{\mathfrak{E}}}
that we work with them later.
Let us start by extending the notion of pure semisimple for a pair of rings.

Definition 5.1
.

Given a bimodule
{\mathbb{M}}
and a sequence
\bar{\varphi}
of formulas, the notation
(\ast)^{\bar{\varphi},{\mathbb{M}}}_{\aleph_{0},\aleph_{0}}
stands for
the following two assumptions:

(a)

\varphi_{n}=\varphi_{n}(x)
is in
{\mathcal{L}}^{cpe}_{\aleph_{0},\aleph_{0}}(\tau_{{\bf R}})
, and

(b)

the sequence
\langle\varphi_{n}({\mathbb{M}}):n<\omega\rangle
is strictly decreasing.

Note that if
\bar{\varphi}
is as above, then it is
(\aleph_{0},\aleph_{0})
-adequate.
Also for simplicity, we can assume that
\varphi_{n+1}(x)\vdash\varphi_{n}(x)
holds for all
n<\omega
.

Definition 5.2
.

The pair
({\bf R},{\bf S})
of rings
is called purely semisimple if
for some bimodule
{\mathbb{M}}^{\ast}

and a sequence

\bar{\varphi}=\langle\varphi_{n}(x):n<\omega\rangle
, the property
(\ast)^{\bar{\varphi},{\mathbb{M}}^{\ast}}_{\aleph_{0},\aleph_{0}}
holds.

Thanks to
Theorem
2.21

a ring
{\bf R}
is not purely semisimple
if and only if the pair

({\bf R},{\bf R})
is purely semisimple.

Definition 5.3
.

(1)

The sequence
\bar{\varphi}:=\langle\varphi_{n}(x):n<\omega\rangle
is called very nice if
it is as in Definition
5.1
and for some

m_{n},k_{\ell}<\omega
and
a_{\ell},b_{\ell,i}\in{\bf R}
we have

(a)

\varphi_{n}(x)=(\exists y_{0},\ldots,y_{k_{n}-1})[\bigwedge\limits_{\ell=0}^{m_{n}-1}a_{\ell}x_{\ell}=\sum\limits_{i<k_{\ell}}b_{\ell,i}y_{i}]
,

(b)

m_{n}<m_{n+1}
,
k_{\ell}\leq k_{\ell+1}
.

(2)

Let
\bar{\varphi}^{1}:=\langle\varphi_{n}^{1}(x):n<\omega\rangle
and
\bar{\varphi}^{2}:=\langle\varphi_{n}^{2}(x):n<\omega\rangle
be two sequences of formulas. By
\bar{\varphi}^{1}\leq\bar{\varphi}^{2}
we mean

(\forall n<\omega)(\exists m<\omega)[\varphi^{2}_{m}(x)\vdash\varphi^{1}_{n}(x)].

(3)

Let
\bar{\varphi}^{1}
and
\bar{\varphi}^{2}
be two sequences of formulas. We say
\bar{\varphi}^{1}
and
\bar{\varphi}^{2}
are equivalent if

\bar{\varphi}^{1}\leq\bar{\varphi}^{2}
and
\bar{\varphi}^{2}\leq\bar{\varphi}^{1}
.

(4)

{\mathfrak{e}}\in{\mathfrak{E}}
is called
\kappa
-simple if for each

n
there are a set
X\subseteq{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
of cardinality

<\kappa
and a set
\Sigma
of

<\kappa
equations
from
\mathcal{L}_{\aleph_{0},\aleph_{0}}(\tau_{{\bf R}})
with parameters from

X

such that
{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
is generated by
X
freely except the
equations in
\Sigma
. We call
X
a witness.

Lemma 5.4
.

Adopt the above notation. The following assertions are true:

(1)

Suppose
(*)^{\bar{\varphi},{\mathbb{M}}^{*}}_{\aleph_{0},\aleph_{0}}
holds. Then there is a very nice sequence
\bar{\varphi}^{\prime}
equivalent to
\bar{\varphi}
.

(2)

If the
\varphi_{n}
’s are from infinitary logic, the same thing holds,
only
m_{n}
,
k_{\ell}
may be infinite but for each
\ell
the set
\{i:b_{\ell,i}\neq 0\}
is finite.

Proof.

We only prove (1), as clause (2) can be proved in a similar way. Thus assume
(\ast)^{\bar{\varphi},{\mathbb{M}}^{\ast}}_{\aleph_{0},\aleph_{0}}
holds. According to Lemma
2.11
,
we can assume that each
\varphi_{n}
is a simple formula, so it is of the form

\varphi_{n}(x)=\left(\exists y_{0},\ldots,y_{k_{n}-1}\right)\left(\bigwedge\limits_{\ell=0}^{m_{n}-1}a^{n}_{\ell}x=\sum\limits_{i<k_{n}}b^{n}_{\ell,i}y_{i}\right),

where
a^{n}_{\ell},b^{n}_{\ell,i}
are members of
{\bf R}
,
k_{n},m_{n}
are
natural numbers.
After replacing
\varphi_{n}
with
\bigwedge\limits_{\ell\leq n}\varphi_{\ell}
, if necessary, we may assume that the sequence is decreasing in the sense that
\varphi_{n+1}(x)\vdash\varphi_{n}(x)
,
for each
n
. Also without loss of generality  and by taking
b^{n}_{\ell,i}=0_{\bf R}
, we can assume that
k_{\ell}<k_{\ell+1}
and
m_{n}<m_{n+1}
. Finally, note that we can even get a better sequence by taking
\varphi_{0}(x)=\exists y_{0}(x=y_{0})
(so

m_{0}=1
,
a_{0}=1_{\bf R}
,
k_{0}=1
and
b_{0,0}=1
). This completes the proof.
∎

Remark 5.5
.

Let
\bar{\varphi}
be a very nice sequence. According to Lemma
5.4
and its proof, we assume from now on that
\varphi_{0}
is of the form
\varphi_{0}(x)=\exists y_{0}(x=y_{0})
.

We now assign to each very nice sequence
\bar{\varphi}
, an element
{\mathfrak{e}}(\bar{\varphi})\in{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{\aleph_{0},\aleph_{0}}

as follows.

Definition 5.6
.

Suppose
\bar{\varphi}
is a very nice sequence. Then

{\mathfrak{e}}(\bar{\varphi})=\langle{\mathbb{N}}_{n},x_{n},g_{n}:n<\omega\rangle

is defined as follows:

•

{\mathbb{N}}_{n}
is the
({\bf R},{\bf S})

bimodule which is generated by

\{x_{n}\}\cup\left\{y_{n,i}:i<k_{m_{n}-1}\right\}

freely except to the equations

\{a_{\ell}x_{n}=\sum\limits_{i<k_{\ell}}b_{\ell,i}y_{n,i}:\ell<m_{n}\}.

In other words,
{\mathbb{N}}_{n}=({\bf R}x{\bf S}\bigoplus\limits_{i<k_{m_{n}-1}}{\bf R}y_{n,i}{\bf S})/{\mathbb{K}}
,
where
{\mathbb{K}}
is the bimodule generated by
\langle a_{\ell}x_{n}-\sum\limits_{i<k_{\ell}}b_{\ell,i}y_{n,i}:\ell<m_{n}\rangle
.

•

g_{n}:{\mathbb{N}}_{n}\to{\mathbb{N}}_{n+1}
is defined so that
g_{n}(x_{n})=x_{n+1}
and
g_{n}(y_{n,i})=y_{n+1,i}
for
i<k_{m_{n}-1}
.

We call
\mathfrak{e}
simple if it is of the form
{\mathfrak{e}}(\bar{\varphi})
for some very nice
\bar{\varphi}.

Note that by Remark
5.5
, for each
n
,
x_{n}=y_{n,0}.

Then next lemma shows that
{\mathfrak{e}}(\bar{\varphi})\in{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{\aleph_{0},\aleph_{0}}

Lemma 5.7
.

Let
\bar{\varphi}
be very nice and set
{\mathfrak{e}}:={\mathfrak{e}}(\bar{\varphi})
. The following assertions are valid:

(1)

x_{n}\in\varphi_{n}({\mathbb{N}}_{n})
.

(2)

Let
{\mathbb{M}}
be a bimodule. Then
x^{\ast}\in\varphi_{n}({\mathbb{M}})
if and only if
for some bimodule homomorphism
h:{\mathbb{N}}_{n}\to{\mathbb{M}}
we have
h(x_{n})=x^{\ast}
.

(3)

x_{n}\notin\varphi_{n+1}({\mathbb{N}}_{n})
.

(4)

Each
g_{n}
is a bimodule homomorphism.

Proof.

Clauses (1), (3) and (4) are trivial. Clause (2)
follows from Lemma
3.8
.
∎

We will frequently use the following simple observation without any mention of it.

Lemma 5.8
.

Let
{\mathfrak{e}}\in{\mathfrak{E}}^{\mathfrak{m}}

be simple. Then for every
n<\omega,

\psi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
and
\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}

are equivalent. In other words, if
{\mathbb{M}}^{\mathfrak{m}}_{*}\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}
and
x\in{\mathbb{M}}
then

{\mathbb{M}}\models
“
\psi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}(x)\leftrightarrow\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}(x)
”.

We now define some very special bimodules.

Definition 5.9
.

Let
n<\omega
.

(1)

Let
{\mathbb{N}}^{\prime}_{n}
be the bimodule generated by
x_{n}
,
y^{\prime}_{n,i}
and

y^{\prime\prime}_{n,i}
for
i<k_{m_{n}-1}
freely subject to the following relations for

\ell<m_{n}
:

(a)

a_{\ell}x=\sum_{i<k_{\ell}}b_{\ell,i}y^{\prime}_{n,i}

(b)

a_{\ell}x=\sum_{i<k_{n}}b_{\ell,i}y^{\prime\prime}_{n,i}.

(2)

Let
{\mathbb{N}}^{\ell}_{n}
for
\ell=1,2
, be the sub-bimodule of
{\mathbb{N}}^{\prime}_{n}
generated by:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\{x_{n}\}\cup\{y^{\prime}_{n,i}:i<k_{m_{n}-1}\}&\qquad\mbox{for}\quad\ell=1,\\
\{x_{n}\}\cup\{y^{\prime\prime}_{n,i}:i<k_{m_{n}-1}\}&\qquad\mbox{for}\quad\ell=2.\end{array}

(3)

Let
f^{\ell}_{n}:{\mathbb{N}}_{n}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{N}}^{\ell}_{n}
be the bimodule homomorphisms
defined by the following assignments:

(a)

f^{\ell}_{n}(x_{n})=x_{n},

(b)

f^{1}_{n}(y_{n,i})=y^{\prime}_{n,i}
and

(c)

f^{2}_{n}(y_{n,i})=y^{\prime\prime}_{n,i}.

(4)

{\mathbb{L}}^{{\mathord{\mathrm{tr}}},\bar{\varphi}}_{n}:=\{z\in\varphi_{n}({\mathbb{N}}_{n}):f^{1}_{n}(z)-f^{2}_{n}(z)\in\varphi_{\omega}({\mathbb{N}}^{\prime}_{n})\}.

Clearly, it is an Abelian subgroup of
{\mathbb{N}}_{n}
.

Let
\mathfrak{e}\in{\mathfrak{E}}
and suppose
\bar{\varphi}
is an adequate sequence for
\mathfrak{e}
.
Also, let
({\bf h_{1}},{\bf h_{2}})
be a pair of bimodule homomorphisms from
{\mathbb{N}}_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}
to
{\mathbb{M}}
. Recall from Definition
3.27
that

{\mathbb{L}}^{{\mathfrak{e}},\bar{\varphi},{\bf h_{1}},{\bf h_{2}}}_{n}=\{z\in\varphi_{n}({\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}):{\bf h_{1}}(z)={\bf h_{2}}(z)\ \mod\ \varphi_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}})\}.

In the case
{\mathfrak{m}}
is a context, we recall

{\mathbb{L}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}[{\mathfrak{m}}]=\bigcap\limits_{{\mathbb{M}}\in\mathcal{K}\cup\{{\mathbb{M}}_{\ast}\}}\big\{{\mathbb{L}}^{\mathfrak{e},\bar{\varphi}^{\mathfrak{e}},{\bf h_{1}},{\bf h_{2}}}_{n}:{\bf h_{1}},{\bf h_{2}}\in\textmd{Hom}({\mathbb{N}}_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}},{\mathbb{M}})\text{~and~}{\bf h_{1}}(x_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}})={\bf h_{2}}(x_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}})\big\}.

In the next lemma we show that the bimodule
{\mathbb{N}}^{\prime}
and the homomorphisms
f^{1}_{n},f^{2}_{n}
are sufficient to determine

{\mathbb{L}}^{\mathfrak{e}(\bar{\varphi})}_{n}[{\mathfrak{m}}]
provided
\bar{\varphi}
is very nice.

Lemma 5.10
.

Let
{\mathfrak{m}}
be a nice context,
\bar{\varphi}
is very nice and

{\mathfrak{e}}:={\mathfrak{e}}(\bar{\varphi})\in{\mathfrak{E}}^{{\mathfrak{m}}}
. Then

{\mathbb{L}}^{{\mathfrak{e}}(\bar{\varphi})}_{n}[{\mathfrak{m}}]={\mathbb{L}}^{\rm tr,\bar{\varphi}}_{n}
.

Proof.

Let
z\in{\mathbb{L}}^{{\mathfrak{e}}(\bar{\varphi})}_{n}[{\mathfrak{m}}]
.
Since
f^{1}_{n},f^{2}_{n}:{\mathbb{N}}_{n}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{N}}^{\ell}_{n}
satisfy
f^{1}_{n}(x_{n})=f^{2}_{n}(x_{n})
, thus it follows from Definition
5.9

that

z\in{\mathbb{L}}^{\rm tr,\bar{\varphi}}_{n}
.

In order to prove
{\mathbb{L}}^{\rm tr,\bar{\varphi}}_{n}\subseteq{\mathbb{L}}^{{\mathfrak{e}}(\bar{\varphi})}_{n}[{\mathfrak{m}}]
,
let
{\bf h_{1}},{\bf h_{2}}:{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}\to{\mathbb{M}}\in{\mathcal{K}}\cup\{{\mathbb{M}}_{\ast}\}
be such that
{\bf h_{1}}(x_{n})={\bf h_{2}}(x_{n})
.
Note that

{\mathbb{M}}\models\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\bf h}_{1}(x_{n}))
and
{\mathbb{M}}\models\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\bf h}_{2}(x_{n}))
,
thus we can find
{\bf h}_{1}^{\prime}:{\mathbb{N}}^{1}_{n}\to{\mathbb{M}}
and
{\bf h}_{2}^{\prime}:{\mathbb{N}}^{2}_{n}\to{\mathbb{M}}
such that

{\bf h}_{1}=f^{1}_{n}\circ{\bf h}_{1}^{\prime}
and
{\bf h}_{2}=f^{2}_{n}\circ{\bf h}_{2}^{\prime}
. Take some
z\in{\mathbb{L}}^{\rm tr,\bar{\varphi}}_{n}
. By definition,

z\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{N}}_{n})
and
f^{1}_{n}(z)-f^{2}_{n}(z)\in\varphi_{\omega}({\mathbb{N}}^{\prime}_{n})
. But then

{\bf h}_{1}(z)-{\bf h}_{2}(z)\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}})
. From this,

z\in{\mathbb{L}}^{{\mathfrak{e}},\bar{\varphi},{\bf h_{1}},{\bf h_{2}}}_{n}
. Since
{\bf h_{1}},{\bf h_{2}}
were be arbitrary,

z\in{\mathbb{L}}^{{\mathfrak{e}}(\bar{\varphi})}_{n}[{\mathfrak{m}}]
.
∎

So, if
{\mathfrak{m}}
is a context whose
{\mathfrak{E}}^{\mathfrak{m}}
consists of simple
\mathfrak{e}
’s and if
{\mathbb{M}}\in\mathcal{K}^{{\mathfrak{m}}}
, then every
{\bf R}
-endomorphism is
in some sense definable, i.e., by fixing
{\mathfrak{e}}\in{\mathfrak{E}}^{\mathfrak{m}}
and restricting ourselves to
\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}})
for large enough
n,
modulo
\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}})
, it is determined by some
z\in{\mathbb{L}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}[{\mathcal{K}}^{\mathfrak{m}}]
. However, not every such
z
may really occur.
We try to formalize this in Lemma

5.22
.

Notation 5.11
.

From now on we fix a context
{\mathfrak{m}}=(\mathcal{K},{\mathbb{M}}_{*},{\mathfrak{E}},{\bf R},{\bf S},{\bf T})
.

Definition 5.12
.

Suppose
{\mathfrak{e}}\in{{\mathfrak{E}}}
,

n<\omega
,
z\in{\mathbb{L}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}

and let
{\mathbb{M}}
be a bimodule which
\leq^{ads}_{{\mathcal{K}},\aleph_{0}}
-extends

{\mathbb{M}}_{\ast}
.
We define
{\bf h}_{{\mathbb{M}},z}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}
as a morphism from the additive group
\psi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}})/\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}})
14
14

14

Pedantically we should write
\psi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}})/\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}})\cap\psi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}}).
to itself so that for every bimodule homomorphism
h:{\mathbb{N}}_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}\to{\mathbb{M}}

{\bf h}_{{\mathbb{M}},z}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}\left(h(x_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}})+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}})\right):=h(z)+\varphi_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}).

Remark 5.13
.

By Lemma
3.8
, every
w\in\psi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}})
is of the form
h(x_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}})
, for some
h:{\mathbb{N}}_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}\to{\mathbb{M}}
as above. Also note that as
z\in{\mathbb{L}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
, if
h^{\prime}:{\mathbb{N}}_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}\to{\mathbb{M}}
is another bimodule
homomorphism such that
h^{\prime}(x_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}})=h(x_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}})
, then
h^{\prime}(z)=h(z)
mod
\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}})
.
This shows that
{\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e},n}_{{\mathbb{M}},z}
is well-defined, and does not depend on the choice of
{h}
.

Definition 5.14
.

Let
{\mathfrak{e}}\in{{\mathfrak{E}}}
,

n<\omega
,
z\in{\mathbb{L}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}

and let
{\mathbb{M}}
be a bimodule such that

{\mathbb{M}}_{\ast}\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}
. Then

(1)

z\in{\mathbb{L}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
is called
({\mathfrak{m}},{\mathfrak{e}},n)
-nice
if when
h:{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{M}}
is a bimodule homomorphism,
m\geq n

and
{\mathbb{M}}\models\psi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{m}(h(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}))
,
then
{\mathbb{M}}\models\psi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{m}(h(z))
.

(2)

z\in{\mathbb{L}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
is called
({\mathfrak{m}},{\mathfrak{e}})
-nice if
it is
({\mathfrak{m}},{\mathfrak{e}},n)
-nice for every
n
.

(3)

z\in{\mathbb{L}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
is called weakly

({\mathfrak{m}},{\mathfrak{e}})
-nice if
there is an infinite
{\mathcal{U}}\subseteq\omega
such that
z

is
({\mathfrak{m}},{\mathfrak{e}},n)
-nice
for every
n\in{\mathcal{U}}
.

We remove
({\mathfrak{m}},{\mathfrak{e}})
, when it is clear from the context.

We now define a subgroup of
{\mathbb{L}}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}_{n}
.

Definition 5.15
.

For each
\mathfrak{e}\in{\mathfrak{E}}
and
n<\omega
, we define

{\mathbb{L}}^{{\mathfrak{e}},\ast}_{n}:=\{z\in{\mathbb{L}}^{{\mathfrak{e}}}_{n}:z\text{~is ~}({\mathfrak{m}},{\mathfrak{e}})\text{-nice}\}.

Lemma 5.16
.

Let
{\mathfrak{e}}
be simple and
{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
be strongly nice
for
(\lambda,{\mathfrak{m}},S,\kappa)
and let
{\mathbf{f}}
be an

{\bf R}
-endomorphism of
{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
. The following assertions hold:

(1)

Let
z
be as Lemma
4.7
. Then

z\in{\mathbb{L}}^{{\mathfrak{e}},\ast}_{n}
.

(2)

For some
n<\omega
and

z\in{\mathbb{L}}^{{\mathfrak{e}},*}_{n}
there is
{\mathbb{K}}^{\prime}\in{\rm cl_{is}}({\mathcal{K}})
such that

{\mathbb{M}}^{\prime}:={\mathbb{M}}_{*}\oplus{\mathbb{K}}^{\prime}\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
and

x\in\psi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa})\Rightarrow{\bf f}(x)+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa})+{\mathbb{M}}^{\prime}={\bf h}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}_{{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa},z}(x)+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa})+{\mathbb{M}}^{\prime}.

Proof.

(1)
: Since
{\mathfrak{e}}
is simple, there is a very nice sequence

\overline{\varphi}
such that
{\mathfrak{e}}={\mathfrak{e}}(\overline{\varphi})
.
Since
z\in{\mathbb{L}}^{{\mathfrak{e}}}_{n}
we know that

z\in\varphi_{n}({\mathbb{N}}^{{\mathfrak{e}}}_{n})
and
f^{1}_{n}(z)-f^{2}_{n}(z)\in\varphi_{\omega}({\mathbb{N}}^{\prime}_{n})
where

f^{\ell}_{n}:{\mathbb{N}}_{n}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{N}}^{\ell}_{n}
are bimodule homomorphisms defined in Definition
5.9
(3).
Now suppose
{\bf h}:{\mathbb{N}}_{n}\to{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
is a bimodule homomorphism,
m\geq n
and suppose

{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}\models\psi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{m}({\bf h}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n})).

We are going to show that

{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}\models\psi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{m}({\bf h}(z)).

Recall that
{\bf f}({\bf h}(x_{n}))-{\bf h}(z)\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}+\psi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa})
.
This gives an element
y\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}
such that

{\bf f}({\bf h}(x_{n}))-{\bf h}(z)+y\in\psi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}).

In particular,
{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}\models\psi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{m}({\bf f}({\bf h}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}))-{\bf h}(z)+y)
.
As
\overline{\varphi}
is very simple,

{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}\models\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{m}({\bf f}({\bf h}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}))-{\bf h}(z)+y).

We conclude from this that there is a bimodule homomorphism
H_{1}:{\mathbb{N}}_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}\to{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
such that

H_{1}(x_{m})={\bf f}({\bf h}(x_{n}))-{\bf h}(z)+y
. Since
{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}\models\psi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{m}({\bf h}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}))
,
we have
{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}\models\psi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{m}({\bf f}({\bf h}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n})))
. This gives a bimodule homomorphism
H_{2}:{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}\to{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
such that

H_{2}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{m})={\bf f}({\bf h}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}))
. Define

K:{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}\to{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
by
K:=H_{2}-H_{1}
. Clearly,
K(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{m})={\bf h}(z)+y
. So,

{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}\models\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{m}({\bf h}(z)+y).

Take
{\mathbb{M}}\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{*}
be such that
{\bf h}(z)\in{\mathbb{M}}
and
{\mathbb{M}}\oplus{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
. Then

{\mathbb{M}}\oplus{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}\models\psi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{m}({\bf h}(z)+y).

So,
{\mathbb{M}}\models\psi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{m}({\bf h}(z))
and then
{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}\models\psi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{m}({\bf h}(z))
, as required.

(2)
: In view of Lemmas
4.2
and
4.7
, there are
n<\omega
and
\alpha\in\kappa\setminus S

such that the property
({\mathord{\mathrm{Pr}}}1)^{n}_{\alpha,z}[{\mathbf{f}},{\mathfrak{e}}]

holds. Since
{\mathfrak{e}}
is simple, by Lemma
5.8
we have

\psi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}})\equiv\psi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}})
.

Now let
x\in\psi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa})
. Then
x\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa})
. This give us a bimodule homomorphism
{\bf h}:{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}\to{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}

such that
{\bf h}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n})=x
.
Since
({\mathord{\mathrm{Pr}}}1)^{n}_{\alpha,z}[{\mathbf{f}},{\mathfrak{e}}]
holds, thus
we have

{\bf f}(x)-{\bf h}(z)={\bf f}({\bf h}(x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}))-{\bf h}(z)\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}).

Also, recall that

{\bf h}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}_{{\mathbb{M}},z}(x+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}))={\bf h}(z)+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}).

This completes the argument.
∎

Remark 5.17
.

In Lemma
5.16
, if
||{\mathbb{M}}_{*}||<\lambda
and
||{\mathbb{K}}||<\lambda

for every
{\mathbb{K}}\in{\mathbb{K}}
,
then
||{\mathbb{M}}^{\prime}||<\lambda=||{\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}||
.

Definition 5.18
.

(1)

Let
{\mathbb{M}}
be an
{\bf R}
-module,
\mathfrak{e}\in{\mathfrak{E}}
and
n<\omega.

(a)

For
{\mathbf{f}}\in{\rm End}({\mathbb{M}})
, we set
\hat{{\mathbf{f}}}_{n}:={\mathbf{f}}\restriction\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}})/\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}).

(b)

Let

{\rm End}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}({\mathbb{M}}):=\ \big\{\hat{{\mathbf{f}}}_{n}:{\mathbf{f}}\in{\rm End}({\mathbb{M}})\big\}
15
15

15

Recall that

{\rm End}({\mathbb{M}}):={\rm End}_{{\bf R}}({\mathbb{M}})
is the ring of endomorphisms of
{\mathbb{M}}
.
.

(c)

Let
\Upsilon_{n,\lambda}({\mathbb{M}})
be the family of all
{\mathbf{f}}\in{\rm End}({\mathbb{M}})

such that for some
A\subseteq{\mathbb{M}}
of
cardinality
<\lambda
we have

\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\mathbf{f}}{\restriction}\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}}))\subseteq\{x+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}):x\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}(\langle A\rangle_{\mathbb{M}})\}
.

(d)

{\rm End}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}_{<\lambda}({\mathbb{M}}):=\ \big\{\hat{{\mathbf{f}}}_{n}\in{\rm End}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}({\mathbb{M}}):{\mathbf{f}}\in\Upsilon_{n,\lambda}({\mathbb{M}})\},

which is a two-sided ideal of
{\rm End}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}({\mathbb{M}})
.

(e)

Let
n\leq m
. The notation
{\bf h}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n,m}_{<\lambda}[{\mathbb{M}}]
stands for the
natural map from

{\rm End}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}_{<\lambda}({\mathbb{M}})
to
{\rm End}^{{\mathfrak{e}},m}_{<\lambda}({\mathbb{M}})
.
In particular,
\{{\rm End}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}_{<\lambda}({\mathbb{M}});{\bf h}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n,m}_{<\lambda}[{\mathbb{M}}]\}

is a directed system.

(f)

{\rm End}^{{\mathfrak{e}},\omega}_{<\lambda}({\mathbb{M}}):=\varinjlim(\cdots\longrightarrow{\rm End}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}_{<\lambda}({\mathbb{M}})\longrightarrow{\rm End}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n+1}_{<\lambda}({\mathbb{M}})\longrightarrow\cdots).

(g)

We denote the natural maps:

{\bf h}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n,\omega}_{<\lambda}[{\mathbb{M}}]:{\rm End}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}_{<\lambda}({\mathbb{M}})\to{\rm End}^{{\mathfrak{e}},\omega}_{<\lambda}({\mathbb{M}}).

(2)

Suppose in addition that
{\mathbb{M}}
is an
({\bf R},{\bf S})
-bimodule.

(a)

For any
{\mathbf{f}}\in{\rm End}_{{\bf R}}({\mathbb{M}})
, we assign
\hat{{\mathbf{f}}}_{n}:={\mathbf{f}}\restriction\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}})/\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}).
16
16

16

There should be no confusion with clause (1)(a) above, as here we are talking about a bimodule
{\mathbb{M}}.

(b)

The ring of
{\bf R}
-endomorphisms of
{\mathbb{M}}
induces the following ring

{\rm End}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n,*}({\mathbb{M}}):=\{\hat{{\mathbf{f}}}_{n}:{\mathbf{f}}\in{\rm End}_{\bf R}({\mathbb{M}})\}.

(c)

Let
\Upsilon^{*}_{n,\lambda}({\mathbb{M}})
be the family of all
{\mathbf{f}}\in{\rm End}_{{\bf R}}({\mathbb{M}})

such that for some
A\subseteq{\mathbb{M}}
of
cardinality
<\lambda
we have
\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\mathbf{f}}{\restriction}\psi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}}))\subseteq\{x+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}):x\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}(\langle A\rangle_{\mathbb{M}})\}
.

(d)

{\rm End}_{<\lambda}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n,*}:=\{\hat{{\mathbf{f}}}_{n}:{\mathbf{f}}\in\Upsilon^{*}_{n,\lambda}({\mathbb{M}})\}.

It is easily seen that all the above defined notions are rings.
We now define expansions of
\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}})/\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}})
.

Definition 5.19
.

Let
{\mathbb{M}},\mathfrak{e}
and
n
be as above.

(1)

The notation

{\mathfrak{B}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}})
stands for

\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}})/\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}})

expanded by the finitary relations definable by formulas in
\mathcal{L}_{\infty,\omega}^{\text{pe}}(\tau_{{\bf R}})

(so actually even
if we use this notation for a bimodule
{\mathbb{M}}
, it counts only as an

{\bf R}
-module).

(2)

Similarly, we define
{}^{+}{\mathfrak{B}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}})
, where p.e. formulas are
replaced by “formulas preserved by direct sums”.

(3)

For a bimodule
{\mathbb{M}}
we define
{\mathfrak{B}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}})
and
{}^{+}{\mathfrak{B}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}})
similarly let restricting ourselves
to
\psi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}})
.

So,

{\mathfrak{B}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}})=(\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}})/\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}),\langle R\rangle_{R\in\mathcal{R}}),

where
\mathcal{R}
consists of all finitary relations defined by a formula from

\mathcal{L}_{\infty,\omega}^{\text{pe}}(\tau_{{\bf R}})
. Similarly

{}^{+}{\mathfrak{B}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}})=(\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}})/\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}),\langle R\rangle_{R\in{}^{+}\mathcal{R}}),

where
{}^{+}\mathcal{R}
consists of all finitary relations defined which are defined by a formula from

\mathcal{L}_{\infty,\omega}(\tau_{{\bf R}})
which is preserved under direct sums.
Since, by Lemma
2.13
, pe-formulas are preserved by direct limits,
{}^{+}{\mathfrak{B}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}})
expands
{\mathfrak{B}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}})
.

Lemma 5.20
.

The following assertions are hold:

(1)

Adopt the notation of Definition
5.18
(1). Then
{\rm End}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}({\mathbb{M}})
is a ring with
1
and

{\rm End}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}_{<\lambda}({\mathbb{M}})
is a two-sided ideal of
{\rm End}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}({\mathbb{M}})
.
Note that this ideal may be proper, i.e.,
1\notin{\rm End}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}_{<\lambda}({\mathbb{M}})
or not.
Suppose in addition
{\mathbb{M}}

is a bimodule, then
{\bf S}
is mapped naturally.

(2)

{\rm End}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}_{<\lambda}({\mathbb{M}})
is a two-sided subideal of
{\rm End}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}_{<\mu}({\mathbb{M}})
when
\lambda<\mu
.

(3)

{\rm End}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}_{<\|{\mathbb{M}}\|^{+}}({\mathbb{M}})={\rm End}^{\mathfrak{e},n}({\mathbb{M}})
.

(4)

If
{\mathbb{M}}_{1},{\mathbb{M}}_{2}
are
{\bf R}
-modules and
{\bf h}
is an
{\bf R}
-homomorphism from
{\mathbb{M}}_{1}
to

{\mathbb{M}}_{2}
, then
{\bf h}

induces a homomorphism from

{\mathfrak{B}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{1})
into
{\mathfrak{B}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{2})
.

Proof.

(1).
It is clear that all the defined notions are rings (not necessarily with
1
).
Now suppose
{\mathbb{M}}
is a bimodule. Then one can easily show that for each

s\in{\bf S}
,

s
defines
an
{\bf R}
-endomorphism of
{\mathbb{M}}
by

x\rightarrow xs.

Items (2) and (3) are clear. To prove (4), first note that, as
{\bf h}(\varphi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{1}))\subseteq\varphi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{2})
,

\hat{\bf h}_{n}={\bf h}\restriction\varphi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{1})/\varphi_{\omega}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{1}):\varphi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{1})/\varphi_{\omega}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{1})\to\varphi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{2})/\varphi_{\omega}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{2})

is well-defined.
Now let
\varphi(\nu_{1},\cdots,\nu_{n})\in\mathcal{L}_{\infty,\omega}^{\text{pe}}(\tau_{{\bf R}}),

and for
\ell=1,2
set

R^{\ell}=\{\langle x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}\rangle\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\ell}:{\mathbb{M}}_{\ell}\models\varphi(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n})\}.

Clearly
{\bf h}(R^{1})=R^{2}
,
and hence
\hat{\bf h}_{n}(R^{1}+\varphi_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}_{1}))=R^{2}+\varphi_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}_{2})
.
The result follows immediately.
∎

Remark 5.21
.

If
{\bf h}
is an
{\bf R}
-homomorphism from
{\mathbb{M}}_{1}
to

{\mathbb{M}}_{2}
and
n<\omega
, then we use
\hat{\bf h}_{n}
for the homomorphism from
{\mathfrak{B}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{1})
into
{\mathfrak{B}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}}_{2})
from Lemma
5.20
(4).

We now define some rings derived from the ring of

{\bf R}
-endomorphism of bimodules. Before doing that we need the following lemma which shows
that the maps
{\bf h}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}_{{\mathbb{M}},z}
from Definition
5.12
are definable.

Lemma 5.22
.

Suppose
{\mathbb{M}}
is a bimodule,
\mathfrak{e}\in{\mathfrak{E}}
is simple and
n<\omega
.

(1)

Suppose
{\bf h}:{\mathbb{M}}_{1}\to{\mathbb{M}}_{2}
is an
{\bf R}
-homomorphism,
\mathfrak{e}\in{\mathfrak{E}}
,
n<\omega

and
z\in{\mathbb{L}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
. Then
{\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e},n}_{{\mathbb{M}}_{1},z}\circ\hat{\bf h}_{n}=\hat{\bf h}_{n}\circ{\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e},n}_{{\mathbb{M}}_{2},z}
.

(2)

Suppose
z\in{\mathbb{L}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
is
n
-nice and
m>n.
Then there is
y\in{\mathbb{N}}_{m}^{\mathfrak{e}}
such that for every bimodule

{\mathbb{M}},

{\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e},m}_{{\mathbb{M}},y}={\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e},n}_{{\mathbb{M}},z}\restriction(\psi_{n}({\mathbb{M}})/\varphi_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}})).

(3)

Suppose
\psi(x,y)\in\mathcal{L}_{\infty,\omega}^{\rm pe}(\tau_{({\bf R},{\bf S})})
is such that

(i)

\psi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}(x)\vdash\exists y\psi(x,y)
,

(ii)

\psi(x,y)\vdash\psi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}(x)~\wedge~\psi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}(y)
,

(iii)

\psi(x,y_{1})\wedge\psi(x,y_{2})\vdash\psi_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{e}}(y_{1}-y_{2}),
for all
\ell<\omega
.

Then there is some
z\in{\mathbb{L}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
such that

(\star)^{n}_{\psi,z}:
for every bimodule
{\mathbb{M}}
and
x,y\in\psi_{n}({\mathbb{M}})
,

{\mathbb{M}}\models\psi(x,y)\iff{\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e},n}_{{\mathbb{M}},z}(x+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}))=y+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}).

(4)

For every
z\in{\mathbb{L}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
, there exists

\psi(x,y)\in\mathcal{L}_{\infty,\omega}^{\rm pe}(\tau_{({\bf R},{\bf S})})
satisfying the above conditions, such that
(\star)^{n}_{\psi,z}
holds.

(5)

If
z_{1},z_{2}\in{\mathbb{L}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n},
then for some
z_{3}\in{\mathbb{L}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
and for all bimodule
{\mathbb{M}},

{\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e},n}_{{\mathbb{M}},z_{3}}={\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e},n}_{{\mathbb{M}},z_{1}}\circ{\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e},n}_{{\mathbb{M}},z_{2}}.

Furthermore,

{\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e},n}_{{\mathbb{M}},z_{1}}\pm{\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e},n}_{{\mathbb{M}},z_{2}}={\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e},n}_{{\mathbb{M}},z_{1}\pm z_{2}}.

(6)

If
z\in{\mathbb{L}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
and
{\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e},n}_{{\mathbb{M}},z}
is a bijection, then for some
z^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{L}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}

and for all bimodule
{\mathbb{M}},

{\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e},n}_{{\mathbb{M}},z^{\prime}}
is the inverse of
{\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e},n}_{{\mathbb{M}},z}
.

(7)

If
{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
is finitely presented, then the formula
\psi(x,y)
is first order.
Furthermore, if
{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
is
generated by
\{y_{i}:i<m_{n}\}
freely except
equations involving
r\in{\bf R}
only
(no
s\in{\bf S}
), and
z\in\sum\limits_{i<m_{n}}{\bf R}y_{i}
, then

\psi(x,y)\in\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\omega}^{\rm pe}(\tau_{{\bf R}})
.

Proof.

(1). Let
{\bf g}:{\mathbb{N}}_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}\to{\mathbb{M}}_{1}
be a bimodule homomorphism and let
x={\bf g}(x_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}})
. Then

{\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e},n}_{{\mathbb{M}}_{2},z}(\hat{\bf h}_{n}(x+\varphi_{\omega}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{1})))={\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e},n}_{{\mathbb{M}}_{2},z}({\bf h}(x)+\varphi_{\omega}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{2})))={\bf h}({\bf g}(z))+\varphi_{\omega}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{2}).

In a similar way, we have

\hat{\bf h}_{n}({\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e},n}_{{\mathbb{M}}_{1},z}(x))=\hat{\bf h}_{n}({\bf g}(z)+\varphi_{\omega}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{1}))={\bf h}({\bf g}(z))+\varphi_{\omega}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}_{2})).

The result follows immediately.

(2). Set
y=g_{n,m}(z)
. It is easily seen that
y
is as required.

(3). Let
z
be such that
\psi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}(x_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}})\vdash\psi(x_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}},z)
. Such a
z

exists by (i), and it is unique mod
\varphi_{\omega}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}})
by (iii).
Now define
{\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e},n}_{{\mathbb{M}},z}
as given. By items (i) and (iii),
{\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e},n}_{{\mathbb{M}},z}

is a well-defined function and by item (ii) we deduce that

{\rm Dom\/}({\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e},n}_{{\mathbb{M}},z})=\psi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}})/\varphi_{\omega}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}),

\mathop{\mathrm{Rang}}({\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e},n}_{{\mathbb{M}},z})=\psi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}})/\varphi_{\omega}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}).

So, we are done.

(4). Suppose
z\in{\mathbb{L}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
is given, and let
{\mathbb{N}}_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}

be as Definition
5.6
. Then for some
r^{*},r^{*}_{i}\in{\bf R}
and
s^{*},s^{*}_{i}\in{\bf S},
for
i<k_{m_{n}-1}
, we have

z=r^{*}x_{n}s^{*}+\sum\limits_{i<k_{m_{n}-1}}r^{*}_{i}y_{n,i}s^{*}_{i}\quad(+)

Let
\psi(x,y)
be the formula

\psi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}(x)~\wedge~\psi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}(y)\wedge y=r^{*}xs^{*}.

We are going to show that
\psi(x,y)
is as required. Clauses (i)-(iii) are clearly satisfied.
To prove
(\star)^{n}_{\psi,z}
, let
{\mathbb{M}}
be a bimodule and
x,y\in\psi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}})
.
Suppose first that
\psi(x,y)
holds. Let also
{\bf g}:{\mathbb{N}}_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}\to{\mathbb{M}}
be a bimodule homomorphism,
defined on generators
x_{n},y_{n,i}
by

{\bf g}(x_{n})=x,\qquad{\bf g}(y_{n,i})=0.

In view of
(+)
we observe that

{\bf g}(z)=r^{*}xs^{*}=y.

According to th definition

{\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e},n}_{{\mathbb{M}},z}(x+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}))=y=\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}).

Conversely, suppose that
{\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e},n}_{{\mathbb{M}},z}(x+\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}))=y=\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}}).

Let
{\bf g}
be defined as above, so that
{\bf g}(x_{n})=x
. Now
it is clear that
{\bf g}(z)=r^{*}xs^{*}=y
,
and thus
\psi(x,y)
holds.

Clauses (5) and (6) follows from a combination of (4) and (5). Clause (7) is clear as well.
The lemma follows.
∎

The above lemma allows us to define
{\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e},n}_{z}
, independent of the choice of the bimodule
{\mathbb{M}}
.

Definition 5.23
.

Suppose
\mathfrak{e}\in{\mathfrak{E}}
is simple as witness by
X
(see Definition
5.3
(4)) and
n<\omega.

(1)

Let
{\bf{D}\bf{E}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
be the following ring, whose universe is:

\{{\bf h}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}_{z}:z\in{\mathbb{L}}^{{\mathfrak{e}}}_{n}\},

such that:

(a)

{\bf h}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}_{z_{1}}={\bf h}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}_{z_{2}}
if and only if

z_{1}-z_{2}\in\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n})
,

(b)

{\bf h}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}_{z_{1}}\pm{\bf h}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}_{z_{2}}={\bf h}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}_{z_{1}\pm z_{2}}
,

(c)

{\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e},n}_{z_{1}}\circ{\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e},n}_{z_{2}}={\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e},n}_{z_{3}}
,
where
z_{3}
is as in Lemma
5.22
(5) ( it is
unique modulo
\varphi_{\omega}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n})
),

(d)

the zero is
{\bf h}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}_{0}
, the one is

{\bf h}_{x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}.

Note that
{\bf{D}\bf{E}}^{n}

is embedded into the endomorphism ring of

\psi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n})/\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{N}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n})

as an Abelian
group.

(2)

{\bf{De}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}:=\{{\bf h}_{z}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}\in{\bf{D}\bf{E}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}:z\in\sum\{{\bf R}x:x\in X\}\}
.

(3)

{\bf{dE}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}:=\big\{{\bf h}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}_{z}\in{\bf{D}\bf{E}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}:{\bf h}_{{\mathbb{M}},z}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}
is an endomorphism of

{\mathfrak{B}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}})
for every bimodule

{\mathbb{M}}
such that
{\mathbb{M}}_{\ast}\leq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}\big\}
,

(4)

{\bf{dE}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n,\ast}:=\{{\bf h}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}_{z}\in{\bf{dE}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}:z
is
n
-nice
\}
.

(5)

{\bf{de}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}=:{\bf{De}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}\cap{\bf{dE}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
,

{\bf{de}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n,\ast}=:{\bf{De}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}\cap{\bf{dE}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n,\ast}
, so they two depend on
X
which
witnesses that
{\mathfrak{e}}
is simple.

(6)

{\bf{D}\bf{E}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\bf R})
is

{\bf{D}\bf{E}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
, when we choose

{\bf S}={\bf T}=\mathop{\mathrm{Cent}}({\bf R})
;
similarly for the others.

In the following diagram we display these rings. By
A\to B
we mean
A
is a subring of
B
:

Lemma 5.24
.

The following assertions are hold:

(1)

{\bf{D}\bf{E}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
is a ring. Furthermore if
\mathfrak{e}
is simple, then
{\bf{De}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}

and
{\bf{dE}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
and

{\bf{de}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}

are subrings of
{\bf{D}\bf{E}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
(all have the unit
1={\bf h}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}_{x^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}}

and zero

{}^{n}{\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{0}
, and extend
{\bf T}
).

(2)

{\bf{De}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
,
{\bf{dE}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
commute. In particular,

{\bf{de}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
is
commutative.

(3)

There is a natural homomorphism from

{\bf{D}\bf{E}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
to
{\bf{D}\bf{E}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n+1}
(
n<\omega
), the direct limit is denoted by
{\bf{D}\bf{E}}^{\mathfrak{e}}
. Similarly for
{\bf{De}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n},{\bf{dE}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
and

{\bf{de}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
,

(4)

The ring
{\bf S}
is
naturally mapped into
{\bf{dE}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
,

(5)

The ring

{\bf{de}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
is naturally embedded into

{\bf{de}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n+1}
and
{\bf{D}\bf{E}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
into

{\bf{D}\bf{E}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n+1}
.

(6)

The Abelian group
\psi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}})/\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}})
is equipped with a
module structure over
{\bf{D}\bf{E}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
and it is naturally a

({\bf{De}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n},{\bf{dE}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n})
–bi module (with
{\bf{de}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}

playing the role of
{\bf T}
).

Proof.

(1). This is clear.

(2). Suppose
z,w\in{\mathbb{L}}^{{\mathfrak{e}}}_{n}
are such that
{\bf h}_{z}^{\mathfrak{e},n}\in{\bf{De}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}

and
{\bf h}_{w}^{\mathfrak{e},n}\in{\bf{dE}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
. Without loss of generality,
z=rx
for some
r\in{\bf R}
and
x\in X
.
Furthermore, since
{\bf h}_{z}^{\mathfrak{e},n}\in{\bf{De}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
, it preserves
pe
-definable relations,
so we can assume without loss of generality that
X=\{x_{n}\}\cup\{y_{n,i}:i<k_{m_{n}-1}\},

the canonical sequence is taken from Definition
5.6
.
Let also

w=r^{*}x_{n}s^{*}+\sum\limits_{i<k_{m_{n}-1}}r_{i}y_{n,i}s_{i}
. We have to show that

{\bf h}_{z}^{\mathfrak{e},n}\circ{\bf h}_{w}^{\mathfrak{e},n}={\bf h}_{w}^{\mathfrak{e},n}\circ{\bf h}_{z}^{\mathfrak{e},n}.

Case 1:

z=ry_{n,i}
for some
i<k_{m_{n}-1}.

In this case, let
\psi_{z}(x,y)=\psi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}(x)\wedge\psi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}(y)\wedge y=0
and
\psi_{w}(x,y)=\psi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}(x)\wedge\psi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}(y)\wedge y=r^{*}xs^{*}
.
It follows from the proof of Lemma
5.22
(4) that, for each
{\mathbb{M}}\geq_{\aleph_{0}}{\mathbb{M}}_{*}
and
x\in\psi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}})
,

{\bf h}_{{\mathbb{M}},z}^{\mathfrak{e},n}(x+\varphi_{\omega}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}))=y+\varphi_{\omega}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}})\iff\psi_{z}(x,y)

and

{\bf h}_{{\mathbb{M}},w}^{\mathfrak{e},n}(x+\varphi_{\omega}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}))=y+\varphi_{\omega}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}})\iff\psi_{w}(x,y).

In particular, we have

{\bf h}_{{\mathbb{M}},w}^{\mathfrak{e},n}({\bf h}_{{\mathbb{M}},z}^{\mathfrak{e},n}(x+\varphi_{\omega}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}})))=y+\varphi_{\omega}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}})

\Updownarrow

\exists v\left(\psi_{z}(x,v)\wedge\psi_{w}(v,y)\right)

\Updownarrow

\exists v\left(\psi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}(x)\wedge\psi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}(v)\wedge\psi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}(y)\wedge v=0\wedge y=r^{*}vs^{*}\right).

Similarly, we have

{\bf h}_{{\mathbb{M}},z}^{\mathfrak{e},n}({\bf h}_{{\mathbb{M}},w}^{\mathfrak{e},n}(x+\varphi_{\omega}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}})))=y^{\prime}+\varphi_{\omega}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}})

\Updownarrow

\exists v\left(\psi_{w}(x,v)\wedge\psi_{z}(v,y^{\prime})\right)

\Updownarrow

\exists v\left(\psi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}(x)\wedge\psi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}(v)\wedge\psi_{n}^{\mathfrak{e}}(y^{\prime})\wedge v=r^{*}xs^{*}\wedge y^{\prime}=0\right).

It follows from the above equations that
y=y^{\prime}=0
, and thus the equality follows.

Case 2:

z=rx_{n}
.

In this case, for each
x\in{\mathbb{M}}
,
{\bf h}_{{\mathbb{M}},z}^{\mathfrak{e},n}(x+\varphi_{\omega}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}))=rx+\varphi_{\omega}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}})
. Set
{\bf h}_{{\mathbb{M}},w}^{\mathfrak{e},n}(x+\varphi_{\omega}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}))=y+\varphi_{\omega}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}).
Then

{\bf h}_{{\mathbb{M}},w}^{\mathfrak{e},n}({\bf h}_{{\mathbb{M}},z}^{\mathfrak{e},n}(x+\varphi_{\omega}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}})))={\bf h}_{{\mathbb{M}},w}^{\mathfrak{e},n}(rx+\varphi_{\omega}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}))=r{\bf h}_{{\mathbb{M}},w}^{\mathfrak{e},n}(x+\varphi_{\omega}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}))=ry+\varphi_{\omega}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}),

and

{\bf h}_{{\mathbb{M}},z}^{\mathfrak{e},n}({\bf h}_{{\mathbb{M}},w}^{\mathfrak{e},n}(x+\varphi_{\omega}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}})))={\bf h}_{{\mathbb{M}},z}^{\mathfrak{e},n}(y+\varphi_{\omega}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}))=ry+\varphi_{\omega}^{\mathfrak{e}}({\mathbb{M}}).

The equality follows in this case as well.

(3).
Recall from Lemma
5.22
(2) that the assignment
{\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e},n}_{{\mathbb{M}},z}\mapsto{\bf h}^{\mathfrak{e},n+1}_{{\mathbb{M}},g_{n}(z)}
defines an embedding

F_{n}:{\bf{D}\bf{E}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}\to{\bf{D}\bf{E}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n+1}

. Now, we define

{\bf{D}\bf{E}}^{\mathfrak{e}}:=\varinjlim({\bf{D}\bf{E}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle F_{0}}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\bf{D}\bf{E}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{1}\longrightarrow\cdots\longrightarrow{\bf{D}\bf{E}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle F_{n}}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\bf{D}\bf{E}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n+1}\longrightarrow\cdots).

Similarly, we define the rings
{\bf{De}}^{\mathfrak{e}},{\bf{dE}}^{\mathfrak{e}}

{\bf{dE}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\ast}
and
{\bf{de}}^{\mathfrak{e}}

by taking them as a direct limit of the corresponding directed system.
In all cases they depend on
{\mathfrak{m}}
of course.

(4). To each
s\in{\bf S}
we assign
{\bf f}_{s}\in{\rm End}({\mathbb{M}})
defined by
{\bf f}_{s}(x)=xs
.
The assignment

defines a map

{\bf S}\longrightarrow{\rm End}({\mathbb{M}})
which is an embedding. For each
n<\omega
, this induces a map

(\hat{{\bf f}_{s}})_{n}:\frac{\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}})}{\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}})}\longrightarrow\frac{\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}})}{\varphi^{\mathfrak{e}}_{\omega}({\mathbb{M}})}.

We may regard this as an endomorphism of
{\mathfrak{B}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}})
.
Now, let
z:=x_{n}s
. Then
(\hat{{\bf f}_{s}})_{n}={\bf h}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}_{z}
.
We proved that the assignment

\rho_{n}:S\to{\bf{dE}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
. Denote the natural map

{\bf{dE}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}\to{\bf{dE}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n+1}
by
H_{n}
.
Now we look at the following commutative diagram:

\begin{CD}{\bf S}@>{=}>{}>{\bf S}@>{=}>{}>\ldots @>{=}>{}>{\bf S}@>{=}>{}>{\bf S}@>{=}>{}>\ldots\\
@V{}V{\rho_{1}}V@V{\rho_{2}}V{}V@V{\ldots}V{}V@V{}V{\rho_{n-1}}V@V{\rho_{n}}V{}V\\
{\bf{dE}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{1}@>{H_{1}}>{}>{\bf{dE}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{2}@>{H_{2}}>{}>\ldots @>{}>{}>{\bf{dE}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n-1}@>{H_{n-1}}>{}>{\bf{dE}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}@>{H_{n}}>{}>\ldots\end{CD}

Taking direct limits of these directed systems, lead us to a natural map

\rho:{\bf S}=\varinjlim{\bf S}\to\varinjlim{\bf{dE}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}={\bf{dE}}^{\mathfrak{e}},

as claimed.

(5). This is similar to (4).

(6). The assignment

The following lemma says that for example for strongly
semi-nice construction
\bar{\mathbb{M}}

we have some control over
{\rm End}_{\bf R}({\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa})
; note
that it only says it is not too large, but we have the freedom to choose the
ring
{\bf S}
in order to make
{\rm End}({\mathbb{M}}_{\lambda})
have some elements with desirable
properties.

Lemma 5.25
.

Suppose
\bar{\mathbb{M}}=\langle{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}:\alpha\leq\kappa\rangle

is a strongly semi-nice
construction for
(\lambda,{\mathfrak{m}},S,\kappa)
,
every
{\mathfrak{e}}\in{{\mathfrak{E}}}

is simple and
{\mathbb{M}}:={\mathbb{M}}_{\kappa}
. The following assertions are hold:

(i)

If
({\mathord{\mathrm{Pr}}}1)^{n}_{\alpha,z}[{\mathbf{f}},{\mathfrak{e}}]
holds, then

{\bf h}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}_{z}
is an endomorphism of
{\mathfrak{B}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}})
. Furthermore
{\bf h}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}_{z}\in{\bf{dE}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n,\ast}
.

(ii)

If
({\mathord{\mathrm{Pr}}}1)^{n}_{\alpha,z}[{\mathbf{f}},{\mathfrak{e}}]
holds
and
{\mathbf{f}}
is an automorphism
of
{\mathbb{M}}
, then
{\bf h}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}_{{\mathbb{M}},z}
is an
automorphism of
{\mathfrak{B}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}})
and even of

{}^{+}{\mathfrak{B}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}({\mathbb{M}})
.

(iii)

{\rm End}^{{\mathfrak{e}},\omega}({\mathbb{M}})/{\rm End}^{{\mathfrak{e}},\omega}_{<\lambda}({\mathbb{M}})
embedded into

{\bf{dE}}^{\mathfrak{e}}
.
Suppose in addition that
\lambda=\kappa
and let
{\bf S}
be a subring of
{\rm End}^{{\mathfrak{e}},\omega}({\mathbb{M}})/{\rm End}^{{\mathfrak{e}},\omega}_{<\lambda}({\mathbb{M}})
of cardinality
<\lambda
. There is a
club
C
of
\kappa
such that for any
\alpha\in C\setminus S

large enough, the ring

{\bf S}
is embedded into

{\rm End}^{{\mathfrak{e}},\omega}({\mathbb{M}}/{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha})
.

(iv)

Let
{\bf h}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}[{\mathbb{M}}]

denote the natural map

{\rm End}^{{\mathfrak{e}},n}({\mathbb{M}})\to{\rm End}^{{\mathfrak{e}},\omega}({\mathbb{M}})
and set
17
17

17
